ll carried the
    // pre-r55 version, where `glyph` was hard-coded to `i === 0 ? '❙❙' : '▶︎'`
    // and no row had a click. So row one showed a PAUSE mark whatever was
    // actually sounding, and pressing any row did nothing — a picture twice
    // over, which is exactly how engineering described it before r55 fixed it in
    // V1 ("the ❙❙ on track 01 was decoration").
    //
    // Same resolution as the album page's tracklist, not a second one, so the
    // drawer row and the album row cannot disagree about what is playing. The
    // markup gets its onClick back too — the key existed here and the hole did
    // not, which is the third shape of today's recurring fault.
    const drawerRecPlaying = st.started && !st.paused && !st.extPlay && st.npPlay === recIdx && recIdx != null;
    const drawerCurTrack = st.npPlay === recIdx ? (st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx) : -1;
    const tracks = TR.map((t, i) => {
      // Same one local as the album page's rows — see hovOn there.
      const colHovOn = st.catHover === recIdx && st.hoverTrack === i;
      return {
      key: 'tk' + i,
      // ── THE STARMAP'S METER LIVES HERE, NOT ON THE STAR (2026-09-10, Tom:
      // "the only exception is the Starmap, where the visualiser happens on the
      // track list in the right panel, as opposed to the star") ───────────────
      // Right, and for a reason worth writing down: a star is a few px of light
      // in a field of them, and hanging a five-bar meter off one would clutter
      // the field and move under the cursor as the pan settles. Hovering a star
      // already lights its track in this column, so the column is where the
      // visitor is looking.
      //
      // ⚠ AND THIS ROW HAD NO HOVER AT ALL — which is why Tom said "it's a
      // little odd they're not there now". The album page's rows got their
      // preview on 09-09 and this builder is a separate one; it was never
      // wired, so the column's tracklist has been silent since hover mode
      // existed. enter/leave here are the same intent gate every other hover
      // surface uses, and hoverTrack names the row so the preview is THAT
      // track rather than the record's first.
      enter: () => { if (recIdx != null) this.hoverEnter({ catHover: recIdx, hoverTrack: i, catHoverTitle: t[0] }); },
      leave: () => this.setState({ catHover: null, hoverTrack: null, catHoverTitle: null }),
      rowTitle: this.playMode(st) ? 'Play this track' : 'Preview — hover to hear',
      vuStyle: this.hoverVuBox(st, colHovOn, { marginLeft: '8px' }),
      vuBars: this.hoverVuBars(5),
      menuOpen: st.trackMenu === i,
      toggle: (e) => { e.stopPropagation(); this.setState({ trackMenu: st.trackMenu === i ? null : i }); },
      ctx: this.openCtx('track', recIdx),
      click: (e) => {
        if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
        if (recIdx == null) return;
        if (drawerRecPlaying && drawerCurTrack === i) { this.setState({ paused: true }); return; }
        this.setState({ trackIdx: i });
        this.play(recIdx, i);
      },
      // Width brought up to the target too, by the same collapse.
        dotsStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', width: '40px', height: '40px', margin: '-8px -9px', borderRadius: '4px', fontSize: '15px', cursor: 'pointer', color: 'var(--ink70)' },
      // The hovered row lights the same way the album page's does, one step
      // below the playing row's wash. This box already had the radius and the
      // negative margins, so the wash reaches the column's gutters as it is.
      box: { display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: '18px 14px 1fr auto 22px', alignItems: 'center', gap: '10px', padding: '8px', margin: '0 -8px', borderRadius: '5px', cursor: this.playMode(st) ? 'pointer' : 'default', background: colHovOn ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.05)' : (i === 0 ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.1)' : 'transparent'), transition: 'background 120ms linear', fontSize: '12px' },
      // REC_TRACKS rows are [title, duration]: the number is the row's own
      // position, not a column in the data.
      num: String(i + 1).padStart(2, '0'),
      numStyle: { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', color: (drawerRecPlaying && drawerCurTrack === i) ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink55)' },
      // V2 · the star drawer's release rows. Same rule as the album tracklist:
      // in hover mode the ▶︎ goes AND its cell goes, so the grid does not keep an
      // empty column. ❙❙ stays on the sounding row — that is state.
      glyph: (drawerRecPlaying && drawerCurTrack === i) ? '❙❙' : (this.playMode(st) ? '▶︎' : ''),
      // ⚠ THE GLYPH GOES, THE CELL STAYS (rule 9, corrected 2026-09-09). This row
      // is a five-track grid — '18px 14px 1fr auto 22px' at box below — and a
      // display:none child is NOT PLACED, so every item after it slid one track
      // left: in hover mode the title landed in the 14px glyph column. Same fault
      // the ATH drop rows had this morning, in the surface next door.
      glyphStyle: (drawerRecPlaying && drawerCurTrack === i)
        ? { width: '16px', flexShrink: 0, fontSize: '10px', color: 'var(--gold)' }
        : (() => {
            const shown = this.playCtl({ width: '16px', flexShrink: 0, fontSize: '10px', color: 'var(--ink55)' });
            return shown.display === 'none'
              ? { width: '16px', flexShrink: 0, fontSize: '10px', visibility: 'hidden', pointerEvents: 'none' }
              : shown;
          })(),
      name: t[0],
      nameStyle: { minWidth: 0, overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis', whiteSpace: 'nowrap', color: 'var(--ink)' },
      time: t[1] || '',
      // Rule 9 again, independently of the glyph: this is the FOURTH of five
      // grid children, so hiding it slid the ⋮ out of its 22px track and left the
      // row's right edge empty. An unknown duration prints nothing (rule 2) but
      // still occupies its cell.
      timeStyle: { visibility: ((!t[1]) || ((this.VPS[this.state.vp] || [])[0] || 1180) < 700) ? 'hidden' : undefined, fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', color: 'var(--ink55)' }
      };
    });

    // A HOVERED ROW IS A HOVERED STAR (2026-09-03, Tom: "it's not just star
    // mouseover, it's album mouseover"). The drawer row and the star are two
    // handles on one record, so hovering either one drives the transport and,
    // with hover audio on, the preview. hoverRow wins only when no star is
    // under the cursor — the field is the more direct gesture.
    // …AND A HOVERED GRID COVER IS TOO (2026-09-03). Same rule on the
    // catalogue: with hover audio on, the cover under the cursor is what the
    // transport names and the site previews. Off, the cover hover is only the
    // lift and the ▶ — the transport stays on what is playing.
    // ── HOVER AUDIO IS ON WHERE THE COVERS ARE THE SUBJECT (2026-09-04) ────
    // Default ON: the star field, the curator map, the drops card and the
    // volumes / releases page. On those four the page IS the catalogue and
    // moving across it is the gesture, so hover audio is the point rather than
    // an opt-in. Everywhere else it stays off until asked for. The visitor's
    // own toggle always wins (st.hoverAudio is not null once they touch it).
    // ⚠ RENAMED 2026-09-04 (A1). This was HOVER_DEFAULT_ON, and the installer's
    // table extractor read the trailing token as a content table called `ON` —
    // an unclassifiable name that refused the whole drop at the gate. It is a
    // LOCAL const, not a table: it is design (which surfaces arm hover audio),
    // it is never injected, and there is nothing for contentTables to say about
    // it. Names inside renderVals now avoid the DEFAULT_ token entirely.
    // ── DOES THIS ROUTE SHOW A TRANSPORT BAR AT ALL? (2026-09-05) ──────────
    // Hoisted out of the hasTransport key so the LAYOUT can ask it too. The
    // deck's nav and its hover switch used to rise on st.started alone, which
    // moved them on a route that never raises a bar — see their notes. The
    // rule itself is unchanged and documented at the hasTransport key below.
    // ── V2 · HOVER MODE HAS NO TRANSPORT AT ALL ────────────────────────────
    // Tom: "in hover mode there would be no transport." This is the gate that
    // makes that true everywhere at once — every offset, reserve and lift in
    // the file already reads hasTransportNow or barUp, so the bar does not just
    // hide: the layout stops reserving for it, the deck nav stops rising for
    // it, and the panes get their space back. That is why the mode goes HERE
    // rather than on transportStyle's display.
    //
    // It reads playMode(st), not st directly, so it is the same one answer the
    // play buttons use — a page cannot have a bar and no buttons, or buttons
    // and no bar.
    const hasTransportNow = !this.playMode(st)
      ? false
      // Nothing to be about, no bar — see hasSubject.
      : !this.hasSubject(st)
      ? false
      // ⚠⚠ ONCE SOMETHING PLAYS, ATH GETS A BAR (2026-09-15, review). This was
      // an unconditional `false`, so the ATH landing could never raise a
      // transport — and today I added a "Play the drop" button and per-track
      // row glyphs to that very surface. Pressing one flipped the label to
      // "❙❙ Pause" and showed the visitor nothing: no artist, no title, no
      // scrub, and no way to pause but the same button. A control that changes
      // state and shows nothing is precisely the dead-control fault the
      // playable-term gates in this file exist to prevent.
      //
      // IT ALSO CONTRADICTED transportStyle, which has always been
      // `(isAth && !st.started) ? 'none' : 'flex'` — written to let the bar
      // appear on ATH once something plays — and the note twelve lines below
      // asserts exactly that ("ATH IS UNAFFECTED and stays quiet:
      // transportStyle's own display gate is (isAth && !st.started)"). Two
      // gates cannot both be the rule.
      //
      // CLAUDE.md settles which: "Landing is a resting state: no transport and
      // no dock UNTIL SOMETHING PLAYS or Releases is opened." So the rule is
      // st.started, not false — quiet at rest, a bar once it sounds. The two
      // gates now share that term, which is this file's own standard:
      // anything that has to clear the bar agrees with the bar.
      : ['ath-map', 'ath-deck'].indexOf(route) > -1
      ? !!st.started
      // ⚠ AND THE SECOND GATE, which is the one Tom actually hit. These routes
      // additionally required something to be PLAYING — so on a landing page,
      // pressing Player left the foot of the page empty even with hasSubject
      // answering yes. An explicit choice satisfies this too; the resting bar
      // names nothing and offers the transport, which is what "now there's a
      // player" should look like.
      //
      // ⚠ ATH IS UNAFFECTED and stays quiet: transportStyle's own display gate
      // is (isAth && !st.started), which is Tom's 09-03 ruling that the player
      // does not exist on Across the Horizon until something is actually
      // playing. That ruling holds and is not re-litigated here.
      : (['landing','catalogue','artist','artists','ath-artists','ath-all-releases','ath-release','ath-track','ath-artist'].indexOf(route) > -1 ? (!!st.started || this.playerChosen(st)) : true);
    // The bar is UP only when the route shows one and something is playing.
    // Every offset that used to clear the transport reads this.
    const barUp = hasTransportNow && !!st.started;
    // Computed here, not inline in the key, because athRelMetaStyle has to know
    // whether it is empty in order to hide itself. One value, two readers.
    // Ported from V1 with relDateLabel (2026-09-09).
    const athTrkMetaText = (() => {
      const d = this.athDrop(st).r, i = st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx;
      const parts = [];
      const rel = this.relDateLabel(d.relDate);
      if (rel) parts.push(rel);
      const trk = this.athDropTrack('Vol. ' + (d.vi + 1) + ' Drop ' + d.num, i);
      if (trk && trk[5]) parts.push(trk[5]);
      return parts.join('  ·  ');
    })();
    // ── IS A NAMED SET OPEN? (2026-09-05) ─────────────────────────────────
    // The results header, its clear chip and the star column all gated on
    // `browseSel` — the browse pick — so ⤢ Open, which opens a CONSTELLATION
    // rather than a facet, landed on a grid that looked exactly like the
    // unfiltered one: no name for the set and no way out of it. Two ways to
    // open a set, one of them invisible.
    // setOpen is the condition all of them read now: a browse pick OR a
    // constellation anchor.
    const setOpen = !!(st.browseSel || st.conAnchor);
    // Hover audio's default lives in ONE place — hoverArmed(). Its note carries
    // the rule and the reversal that set it: ATH on, any star field on, the
    // labels' flat pages off. Nothing here reads a route to decide it.
    const hoverAudioNow = this.hoverArmed(st);
    const hoverPair = (rec) => ({ rec: rec, id: 's' + rec, artist: (this.data('PAIRS')[rec] || [])[0], title: (this.data('PAIRS')[rec] || [])[1] });
    // The same recovery as the arrival branch (interim 3): an id past the
    // painted cache still resolves, because the id carries its record.
    const npStar = st.hoverStar != null ? (stars.find(s => s.id === st.hoverStar) || hoverPair(parseInt(String(st.hoverStar).slice(1), 10)))
      : (st.hoverRow != null ? (stars.find(s => s.rec === st.hoverRow) || hoverPair(st.hoverRow))
      : (st.catHover != null && hoverAudioNow && route !== 'star') ? hoverPair(st.catHover)
      // A hovered face or cover with no record behind it (yet): the transport
      // still names the artist so the gesture is visible; nothing previews
      // (hoverRec stays null) because there is nothing to play.
      : (st.catHoverName && hoverAudioNow && route !== 'star') ? { rec: null, id: null, artist: st.catHoverName, title: st.catHoverTitle || '' } : null);
    // A star under the cursor wins (hover audio is playing it); otherwise the
    // record the visitor actually pressed play on; otherwise the lock. Without
    // the middle term the transport named the locked record while a different
    // one was playing from the grid.
    // ══ NOTHING PLAYING RESOLVES TO null, NEVER TO RECORD 0 (2026-09-09) ═════
    // ⚠ THIS IS THE FALLBACK BEHIND EVERY WRONG-ATTRIBUTION BUG TODAY, and I
    // named it as the real fix last round and then did not make it. It ended
    // `: 0`, so "nothing is playing" was indistinguishable from "record 0 is
    // playing", and every downstream key inherited a record nobody chose:
    // npPair, npTitle, npArtist, npCoverStyle and the bar's ⋮.
    //
    // Visible with ZERO interaction on the Radio page in player mode, which is
    // not in hasTransportNow's started-gated list — so the bar paints on arrival
    // and read "Passing Fires · Passing Fires / Jeremy Cunningham" over silence,
    // with a live ⋮ offering "Open the record".
    //
    // THE EMPTY PATH ALREADY EXISTS. npTitle, npArtist and npCoverStyle all
    // branch on npPair being falsy and print the italic "artist · title from
    // catalogue" placeholder and the hatch — the honest rendering, written for
    // exactly this case and unreachable while the fallback manufactured a
    // record. Rule 4's shape: no value means null, never a stand-in.
    const npRec = npStar ? npStar.rec : (st.npPlay != null ? st.npPlay : (st.lock != null ? st.lock : null));
    const npPair = npRec != null ? this.data('PAIRS')[npRec] : null;
    // ── AND WHAT IS CUED (2026-09-10) ────────────────────────────────────────
    // Only when a bar is actually present, nothing has been pressed, and the
    // queue is genuinely empty. Gated on hasTransportNow — the same lever the
    // landing lift uses two fixes above, and deliberately NOT a fourth answer
    // to "is a bar showing": the file's own rule is that these must share
    // every term or the layout disagrees with the chrome.
    // ⚠ TWO RULES, TWO GATES (2026-09-10). These were one condition and the
    // queue term silently killed the bar: with rows already in QUEUE,
    // `qMerged.length === 0` was false, so the bar fell back to the italic
    // stand-in on a visit where the queue was doing fine. They are separate
    // asks — "it should just load up the song" is about the BAR and holds
    // whenever a bar is present with nothing playing; "it should never be an
    // empty queue" is about the QUEUE and is the only one emptiness gates.
    const cueForBar = (!st.started && hasTransportNow && npRec == null)
      ? this.cueSet(st)
      : [];
    const cueRows = (cueForBar.length && qMerged.length === 0) ? cueForBar : [];
    // What the bar NAMES. npPair stays the authority on what is PLAYING — every
    // playback path still reads it — and this is display only, so a cued record
    // can be named without the transport claiming it is sounding.
    const barPair = npPair || (cueForBar.length ? this.data('PAIRS')[cueForBar[0][4]] : null);
    const cueSecs = cueForBar.length ? cueForBar[0][2] : null;
    // The queue as shown, with the cue appended when there was nothing in it.
    const qShown = qList.concat(cueRows);
    // DOES THE PLAYER ACTUALLY RENDER? One expression, so anything that has to
    // clear the bar agrees with the bar. Mirrors hasTransport's resting-state
    // rule and transportStyle's display gate — the two halves used to be read
    // separately, which is how a page could reserve space for a bar that was
    // not there, or fail to reserve for one that was.
    // THE CURATOR MAP NEVER SHOWS THE TRANSPORT (Tom, 2026-09-03). Hover audio
    // works there through its own On/Off switch; the sound is the site's; no bar.
    // ath-map is back on its ORIGINAL rule here (!!st.started): barShown also
    // drives the curator map's pane reserve and fit geometry, and changing it
    // shifted the map's spacing (Tom, 2026-09-03: "put the curator map back the
    // way it was"). The bar itself is suppressed on the map by hasTransport.
    // ⚠ AND THE SAME SECOND GATE AS hasTransportNow (2026-09-10). This read
    // !!st.started while hasTransportNow reads (!!st.started || playerChosen),
    // so on these ten routes CHOOSING Player painted a resting bar and this
    // reserved nothing for it — flatPaneStyle stayed at 24px and the last rows
    // of the pane sat under the bar with no scroll left to reach them. That is
    // the divergence this key's own comment below forbids: "anything that has
    // to clear the bar agrees with the bar". The two now share every term.
    const barShown = ((route === 'ath-map' || route === 'landing' || route === 'catalogue' || route === 'artist' || route === 'artists' || route === 'ath-artists' || route === 'ath-all-releases' || route === 'ath-release' || route === 'ath-track' || route === 'ath-artist') ? (!!st.started || this.playerChosen(st)) : true)
      && !((isAth && !st.started) || clsPage != null || isGroup)
      // ⚠ THE SAME THIRD TERM. This key's own comment states the rule it was
      // breaking — "One expression, so anything that has to clear the bar agrees
      // with the bar" and "No bar, no reserve" — and its route list returned
      // true unconditionally for radio, record, tour and news, so flatPaneStyle
      // held 104px of dead padding on four routes where no bar paints. Same
      // subject test as hasTransportNow and starTailPx; the three now differ
      // only where they are meant to (the curator map's own spacing ruling).
      && this.hasSubject(st);
    // ── THE BAR'S BAND, ONCE (2026-09-14 audit) ─────────────────────────────
    // transportStyle is `bottom: narrow ? 12 : 20` and `height: narrow ? 76 :
    // 58`, so the band it occupies is 88 on a phone and 78 on a desktop.
    //
    // ⚠ FOUR DIFFERENT NUMBERS WERE CLEARING ONE BAR: 88, 96, 104 and 118. The
    // audit found catPadStyle reserving 96 on narrow, derived in its own
    // comment as "66 + 30" — 66 being the band of the SINGLE-ROW bar, before
    // the mobile pass wrapped it to two rows. So the catalogue grid's last row
    // sat 22px under the transport on every phone: the same fault as the
    // landing clip and the star drawer, for the third and fourth time, because
    // each site held its own literal.
    //
    // My own r74 note says "the bar's height, its inset and everything
    // clearing it are ONE measurement". A literal cannot honour that. This
    // const can, so the three consumers now DERIVE:
    //   drawerStyle   barBand        · the sheet ENDS at the bar, no air
    //   flatPaneStyle barBand + 16   · a pane clears it with the standard air
    //   catPadStyle   barBand + 30/40 · the grid's own heavier tail
    // Change the bar and all three follow. Leave this and nothing drifts.
    const barBand = narrow ? 88 : 78;
    // The record under the finger, if any. Declared here because the card's
    // gate, its own copy and the release list's gate all read it.
    const starCardRec = npStar && npStar.rec != null ? npStar.rec : null;

    const starCardPair = starCardRec != null ? this.data('PAIRS')[starCardRec] : null;
    // A TRANSIENT hover, not a locked record: `view` already flips to 'album'
    // whenever a star is under the pointer, which on a phone handed the drawer a
    // full album panel you could only see the top of. On narrow that hover gets
    // the card instead; a locked star (st.view === 'album') still gets the page.
    const starHoverNarrow = narrow && st.hoverStar != null && st.view !== 'album';
    // The held star's own record, and the View that opens it. Locked or merely
    // hovered, the card reads the same source.
    // ── VIEW OPENS THE FULL ALBUM CARD (2026-09-05, Tom) ────────────────────
    // openAlbum() lands in the star COLUMN, which on a phone is a half-height
    // sheet — so pressing View on the star card swapped one compact card for
    // another compact card in a sheet you then had to drag open. The card's
    // whole job is to say "there is more here", and View has to deliver the
    // more.
    // On narrow it goes to the record ROUTE: the full album page, which is the
    // full album card and is already laid out for a phone. On desktop the
    // column IS the full panel, so that path is unchanged.
    const starCardOpen = () => {
      if (starCardRec == null) return;
      if (narrow) {
        // ── OPEN MEANS THE FULL CARD *IN* THE DRAWER (2026-09-06, Tom) ───────
        // ⚠ I OVER-CORRECTED IN r27. The complaint then was that View swapped
        // one compact card for another compact card you had to drag open, and
        // I fixed it by navigating to the record ROUTE — which threw away the
        // drawer, the star field and the preview card, i.e. the relationship
        // Tom actually likes. The real fault was only that the sheet stayed at
        // half; the destination was right all along.
        //
        // So: the album panel, in the drawer, with the sheet taken to FULL in
        // the same gesture. \`view: 'album'\` is what isAlbum reads, and
        // starHoverNarrow already stands down when the view is album, so the
        // preview card yields to the full card rather than fighting it.
        // Closing (starCardClose) puts the preview back.
        this.setState({
          view: 'album', prev: (st.prev || st.view) === 'album' ? 'releases' : (st.prev || st.view),
          lock: starCardRec, recIdx: starCardRec,
          sheetStep: 'full', open: true
        });
        return;
      }
      this.setState({ starLock: null, hoverStar: null });
      this.openAlbum(starCardRec, (st.prev || st.view) === 'album' ? 'releases' : (st.prev || st.view));
    };
    // Back to the preview card, not out of the star view: the held star stays
    // held, so the compact card returns exactly as it was (2026-09-06).
    const starCardClose = () => {
      this.setState({
        view: (st.prev && st.prev !== 'album') ? st.prev : 'releases',
        lock: null, sheetStep: 'half'
      });
    };
    // ── THE RECORD'S OWN COVER (2026-09-11, Tom) ─────────────────────────
    // This was the artist's portrait from INDEX, landscape-cropped, on the
    // reasoning that the transport showed the cover anyway. Both halves were
    // wrong: the transport only carries a cover while something is PLAYING, so
    // at rest the card had no artwork at all — and CLAUDE.md is explicit that a
    // portrait belongs to a person, never a substitute. A card about a record
    // shows the record.
    //
    // coversFor(pair) is the resolver the record page uses, so the card and the
    // page can never disagree about which image a record has. No cover yet →
    // the striped placeholder, never a portrait standing in.
    const starCardCover = (() => {
      if (!starCardPair) return '';
      const set = this.coversFor(starCardPair);
      return set.length ? set[0] : '';
    })();
    // The header must NOT follow a transient hover: it keeps saying Releases /
    // 179 albums while the card comes and goes underneath it.
    const viewHdr = starHoverNarrow ? st.view : view;

    // ── THE ARTIST PROFILE'S HEAD (2026-09-15, Tom: "if I click on an artist
    // page, it shows me the artist profile in the sidebar") ──────────────────
    // Name over "Artist · <place>", so the head answers who and where before
    // the body answers anything else. Place comes from INDEX[4] where it is
    // known and is simply omitted where it is not — an artist with no recorded
    // city gets "Artist", not "Artist · ".
    const apName = st.artist || this.curArtist();
    const apRow = (this.data('INDEX') || []).filter(a => a && a[0] === apName)[0] || null;
    const apPlace = apRow && apRow[4] ? String(apRow[4]) : '';
    const titles = viewHdr === 'artist'
      ? [apName || 'Artist', 'Artist' + (apPlace ? ' · ' + apPlace : '')]
      : viewHdr === 'album'
      ? [pair ? pair[1] : 'Record ' + String((recIdx != null ? recIdx : 0) + 1).padStart(3, '0'), pair ? pair[0] : 'artist pending']
      : viewHdr === 'results'
      // THE MATCHED COUNT IS THE SITE'S, NOT THE DROP'S (2026-09-04). This line
      // printed a literal 12 over any filter, so picking Ambient said
      // "12 records" above a 98-row list. FILTER_COUNT is the real matched
      // figure; with none, the painted window is the honest fallback.
      ? (() => {
          const fc = this.data('FILTER_COUNT');
          const n = (typeof fc === 'number') ? fc : this.shownCount('filtered');
          return [st.resultLabel || '', (st.resultCat || '') + ' · ' + n + (narrow ? ' albums' : ' records')];
        })()
      // ── THE HEADER IS THE CARD'S OWN (2026-09-09, Tom: the first card "says
      // Curator Map on the top, and it shouldn't, because it's not the curator
      // map"). The column is one view holding eight pages, so the view's title
      // stopped being the answer: each card names itself from ATH_COL, which is
      // also what the menu indexes into.
      : viewHdr === 'ath-curator'
      ? (() => {
          const c = this.ATH_COL[Math.max(0, Math.min(this.ATH_COL.length - 1, st.athCard || 0))];
          return [c[0], colOwner + ' · ' + c[1]];
        })()
      // Whose catalogue, not always the label switcher's pick: arriving from ATH
      // the column has to say Across the Horizon.
      : [this.titlesFor(viewHdr)[0], narrow
          ? (colOwner + ' · ' + (this.titlesFor(viewHdr)[2] || this.titlesFor(viewHdr)[1]))
          : (colOwner + ' · ' + this.countLine(viewHdr, this.shownCount(viewHdr)))];

    const mark = (rawUrl, size, px, color) => ({ /* res(): see the note on res() */
      width: px + 'px', height: px + 'px', flexShrink: 0, cursor: 'pointer',
      backgroundColor: color || 'var(--ink)',
      WebkitMaskImage: 'url(' + this.res(rawUrl) + ')', maskImage: 'url(' + this.res(rawUrl) + ')',
      WebkitMaskRepeat: 'no-repeat', maskRepeat: 'no-repeat',
      WebkitMaskPosition: 'center', maskPosition: 'center',
      WebkitMaskSize: size, maskSize: size
    });

    // EXPLORE MARKS ARE IMAGES, NOT MASKS (2026-09-03, Tom: "the Haulix one is
    // the only one that looks sharp"). Haulix was the only row drawn as a plain
    // background-image; the other seven were CSS masks, and mask rendering is
    // soft at small sizes. Every mark is now a PNG pre-rendered at 3× for the
    // exact size it draws at — one per ink, because the sources are white SVGs
    // and black PNGs and neither works as an image in both themes.
    //
    // Sizes are EQUAL AREA, not equal box: sqrt(w·h) held to 30, so a wide mark
    // (NNA 46×20) and a tall one (Egghunt 26×35) carry the same ink. Files live
    // in brand/marks/explore/<name>-<ink>.png. Regenerate them from the source
    // marks if a mark changes; do not hand-edit.
    const XM = {
      'brand/marks/northern-spy.svg': ['northern-spy', 31, 29], 'brand/marks/nna-tapes.svg': ['nna-tapes', 46, 20],
      'brand/marks/egghunt.svg': ['egghunt', 26, 35], 'brand/marks/whited-sepulchre.png': ['whited-sepulchre', 27, 33],
      'brand/marks/across-the-horizon.png': ['across-the-horizon', 31, 29], 'brand/marks/clandestine.svg': ['clandestine', 29, 31],
      'brand/marks/stellar-atlas.png': ['stellar-atlas', 31, 29],
      // Haulix's source is WHITE ink on transparent — as a plain image it was
      // invisible in light mode. Same per-ink treatment as the rest.
      'public/brand/software/haulix-mark.png': ['haulix', 30, 30]
    };
    const xmark = (rawUrl, _ar, px) => {
      const m = XM[rawUrl]; if (!m) return mark(rawUrl, 'contain', px);
      const ink = st.theme === 'light' ? 'dark' : 'light';
      // The box is 46 wide for EVERY row — the widest normalised mark (NNA) —
      // so a background never clips and the name column stays aligned. Height
      // stays px (36), which clears the tallest mark (Egghunt, 35).
      return { width: '46px', height: px + 'px', flexShrink: 0, cursor: 'pointer',
        backgroundImage: 'url(' + this.res('brand/marks/explore/' + m[0] + '-' + ink + '.png') + ')',
        backgroundSize: m[1] + 'px ' + m[2] + 'px', backgroundPosition: 'center', backgroundRepeat: 'no-repeat' };
    };

    const pill = (on) => ({

      background: on ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.14)' : 'transparent',
      color: on ? '#DBBF3D' : 'rgba(243,241,234,0.6)'
    });

    // ── THE PREVIEW COVER'S SIZE, RESOLVED PER SURFACE (2026-09-11) ────────
    // ⚠ clamp(100px, 18vh, 150px) ALONE WAS WRONG IN THE DESIGN TOOL, and
    // wrong in the direction that hid the feature: vh resolves against the
    // TOOL WINDOW, not the simulated phone frame, so at a 540px window every
    // frame — SE, 390, Pro Max — rendered the cover at the 100px FLOOR. The
    // one thing Tom asked for ("the size of the cover could be dynamic,
    // depending on the size of the screen") was invisible on the only surface
    // he reviews it on, and it showed the SMALLEST cover everywhere, which is
    // the opposite of what the change was approved to do.
    //
    // ONE FORMULA, TWO RESOLVERS — 18% of the viewport height, floor 100,
    // ceiling 150:
    //   · SITE MODE keeps the CSS clamp. The real viewport IS the browser, and
    //     CSS tracks it natively — including an iOS chrome that collapses on
    //     scroll without always firing resize. No JS, no re-render, no stale
    //     value.
    //   · TOOL MODE computes the same number in px from VPS[st.vp][1], the
    //     frame's own height, because vh cannot see a fixed-size simulated
    //     frame. Switching the viewport picker now changes the cover, which is
    //     exactly how this gets reviewed.
    // 100 is where a cover still reads as a cover; 150 is where the text
    // column beside it stops having room for four lines and a 44px action row.
    const starCardCoverSize = this.siteMode()
      ? 'clamp(100px, 18vh, 150px)'
      : Math.max(100, Math.min(150, Math.round(((this.VPS[st.vp] && this.VPS[st.vp][1]) || 844) * 0.18))) + 'px';

      display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center',
      // ⚠ THE TINT WAS PAINTING OVER THE SHELL'S BORDER (2026-09-10, Tom: "the
      // highlight button is over the lines"). The side is 40px for the touch
      // target inside a 34px shell, so its background overflowed the frame top
      // and bottom. background-clip: content-box paints the CONTENT box only —
      // 26px, inside the border — while the element stays 40px and fully
      // tappable. The target and the paint stop being the same box.
      height: '40px', margin: '-7px 0', padding: '7px 0', boxSizing: 'border-box',
      backgroundClip: 'content-box', WebkitBackgroundClip: 'content-box',
      borderRadius: '4px', flex: '1 1 0', minWidth: 0, whiteSpace: 'nowrap',
      fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '10px',
      letterSpacing: '0.1em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
      // ⚠ backgroundColor, NOT background. The SHORTHAND resets background-clip

            const asLabel = (this.LABELS[st.label == null ? 0 : st.label] || [])[4] || 'northern-spy';
            // … except the two stop frames, whose whole point is that they wear
            // whichever site the visitor was on — including ATH, which has no
            // index in that table. They carry; everything else switches.
            const stopRow = parts[1] === 'notfound' || parts[1] === 'error';
            return parts[0] === 'a' ? this.goAth(parts[1])
              : parts[0] === 'r' ? this.goRoute(parts[1], stopRow ? this.labelKeyOf(st) : asLabel)
              : this.go(parts[1], false, asLabel);
          }
        };
      }),

      // THE FRAME IS THE TOOL'S, THE PAGE IS THE DESIGN'S.
      // Tool: a W×H artboard floating on the canvas — 10px corners, a drop
      // shadow, scaled down to fit whatever room the canvas has.
      // Site: the window. No radius, no shadow, no scale, no gutter. Anything
      // else leaves a band of bare page around the site with the corners
      // showing it through, which is exactly what Tom saw.
      // The note under the artboard explaining the drop. Tool chrome.
      // Which world the tool is showing — the real snapshot's stamp, or "mock".
      // Guarded (2026-09-06): dataStamp() returns null before the package
      // loads, and this is tool chrome — it must never be able to throw.
      dataWorld: (() => {
        try {
          const k = this.dataStamp();
          return k ? ('catalogue ' + k) : 'mock data — no data-package.json beside this file';
        } catch (e) { return 'mock data'; }
      })(),
      captionStyle: site ? { display: 'none' } : {
        width: '1180px', maxWidth: '94vw', fontSize: '11px', lineHeight: 1.7,
        color: 'rgba(243,241,234,0.42)', textWrap: 'pretty'
      },
      // The dark ground the artboard floats on — and in site mode, nothing:
      // the stage IS the page, so the shell must not add height, colour or air.
      canvasStyle: site
        ? { minHeight: 0, background: 'transparent', fontFamily: 'Archivo, Helvetica, sans-serif', display: 'block', padding: 0, boxSizing: 'border-box' }
        : { minHeight: '100vh', background: '#101215', fontFamily: 'Archivo, Helvetica, sans-serif', display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', alignItems: 'center', gap: '14px', padding: '14px 0 40px', boxSizing: 'border-box' },
      // The viewport / route picker. It is how I check the design at each
      // declared width; it is not a control the site has.
      pickerStyle: site ? { display: 'none' } : {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '8px', flexWrap: 'wrap', justifyContent: 'center',
        padding: '8px 12px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '7px',
        background: 'rgba(24,27,31,0.9)', border: '1px solid rgba(243,241,234,0.14)',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.14em',
        textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink70)'
      },
      scaleWrapStyle: (() => {
        // W×H IN PIXELS, NOT 100%. Percentages resolve against whatever the
        // host gives this element, and a host that sizes itself to its content
        // gives zero — measured 924×0. W and H already ARE the window
        // (clientWidth/clientHeight, tracked on resize), and every rule inside
        // is computed against them, so the box must be exactly them. It also
        // avoids 100vh, which lies on mobile while a toolbar is retracting.
        if (site) return { position: 'relative', width: W + 'px', height: H + 'px' };
        const s = Math.min(1, (st.avail || W) / W);
        return { position: 'relative', flexShrink: 0, width: (W * s) + 'px', height: (H * s) + 'px' };
      })(),
      stageStyle: (() => {
        const base = {
          overflow: 'hidden', background: 'var(--bg)', color: 'var(--ink)',
          fontFamily: 'Archivo, Helvetica, sans-serif'
        };
        if (site) return Object.assign({ position: 'absolute', top: 0, left: 0, right: 0, bottom: 0 }, base);
        const s = Math.min(1, (st.avail || W) / W);
        return Object.assign({
          position: 'absolute', top: 0, left: 0, width: W + 'px', height: H + 'px',
          transform: 'scale(' + s + ')', transformOrigin: 'top left',
          borderRadius: '10px', boxShadow: '0 30px 80px rgba(0,0,0,0.5)'
        }, base);
      })(),

      // The row's own name for the label: the short form where the row is tight,
      // the full name everywhere else. ONE key, so the header cannot show one
      // and the sheet another — every other reader of the name still gets
      // siteName, which is unchanged.
      labelName: wordmarkShort ? shortName : siteName,

      // ── THE MAP KEEPS THE LABEL YOU ARRIVED ON (2026-09-01) ───────────────
      // REVERSES the atlas-mark-only header below, which was right about the
      // field and wrong about the visitor. The reasoning was that the map is
      // label-agnostic — it shows whatever catalogue you asked for — so a label
      // lockup contradicts the thing on screen. True of the FIELD, and beside

      //
      // No isStar branch: the map now falls through to the same label lockup

      lockMark: athBig

            : {

      lockWrapStyle: (narrow && !onPhoto && !athBig)
        ? { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '10px', flex: 1, minWidth: 0, cursor: 'pointer' }
        : athBig
        ? { display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', alignItems: 'center', gap: '30px', flexShrink: 0, width: '124px', cursor: 'pointer' }
        : athSite
        ? { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '12px', flexShrink: 0, cursor: 'pointer' }
        : (onPhoto && narrow)

        : onPhoto

      wordmarkStyle: (narrow && !onPhoto && athSite)

        : onPhoto
        ? Object.assign(
            { fontFamily: 'Archivo, Helvetica, sans-serif', fontSize: narrow ? '15px' : '22px', fontWeight: 500, lineHeight: 0.94, letterSpacing: '0.005em', textTransform: 'uppercase', textAlign: 'center', color: '#f3f1ea', textShadow: '0 2px 14px rgba(0,0,0,0.65)' },
            // A long name must give way rather than run into the controls:
            // "Whited Sepulchre" and "Across the Horizon" are both longer than

            narrow
              ? { minWidth: 0, maxWidth: '100%', overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis', whiteSpace: 'normal', overflowWrap: 'break-word' }
              : { whiteSpace: 'nowrap' }
          )
        // ATH's name is nearly twice Northern Spy's length, so it carries a
        // smaller wordmark and tighter tracking — that is where the row's extra
        // width comes from, rather than shaving gaps.
        : (athSite && st.theme !== 'dark') ? { display: 'none' }
        // ── THE WORDMARK IS WHAT THE ROW SACRIFICES (2026-09-09, Tom) ─────────
        // "The Explore menu can never be cut off. Browse to Explore always needs
        // to show. On smaller screens you get rid of the Northern Spy text and
        // just keep the logo mark."
        //
        // A ruling, and it settles a question I had been answering wrongly one
        // control at a time: when the row runs out of width, WHICH thing gives
        // way? I had been shaving the mode labels, then their padding, then the
        // gaps — protecting the name and letting the controls fight over what
        // was left, which is how Explore ended up under the drawer.
        //
        // The name is the one thing in this row that is not a control. It is
        // already on screen as the mark beside it, and the mark alone is the
        // label's own identity — so the text is the redundant element and it
        // goes first, everywhere the row is tight. THE CHROME IS NEVER
        // SACRIFICED: ≡, Browse, View, Audio, theme, cart, Explore all survive
        // at every desktop width.
        //
        // ⚠ ATH ALREADY DID THIS and it is where the rule comes from — its mark
        // reads "Across the Horizon", so the text beside it was saying the name
        // twice (see the narrow branch above, and the dark-theme branch on the
        // line before this one). This generalises that decision rather than
        // inventing one.
        //
        // ~175px at 18px for "NORTHERN SPY", against the ~80px the mode labels
        // were buying — so this frees more than twice what shaving them did, and
        // the labels come back at snug as a result.
        // ── SNUG CARRIES THE SHORT FORM (2026-09-09, Tom) ─────────────────────
        // This was display:none — the row sacrificed the name outright. Tom's
        // short forms are the better trade: NSPY at 18px is ~62px against
        // "NORTHERN SPY"'s ~175, so it costs 62 of the 175 the row needed and
        // still says whose site this is. See SHORT_NAME for why the strings are
        // authored rather than derived.
        //
        // Same size and weight as the long form, deliberately — a smaller
        // acronym would read as a subtitle rather than as the wordmark. It is
        // the same lockup, shorter.
        // The wordmark is ALWAYS drawn on desktop now — what changes is the text
        // it carries (see labelName / wordmarkShort). It used to disappear at
        // snug; Tom's ruling is that the name is the last thing to go, so the
        // short form takes over instead and nothing is ever nameless.
        : athSite ? { fontFamily: 'Archivo, Helvetica, sans-serif', fontSize: '13px', fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: '0em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink)', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' }
        : { fontFamily: 'Archivo, Helvetica, sans-serif', fontSize: '18px', fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: '0.02em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink)', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' },
      // The map carries the wordmark too, now — see lockMark. It is the label's
      // name beside the label's mark, the same lockup as every flat page.
      chromeHeaderBar: !onPhoto && !athBig && clsPage == null,
      // The two opening cards are pure statement — no player. It appears from
      // the curator map onward, where hover audio matters.
      // NEVER on a landing page until playback has started: a landing is a
      // statement, and the player only appears once someone hits play. Every
      // other route carries it as normal.
      // THE GRID IS A RESTING STATE TOO (2026-09-01). Arriving at an album grid
      // shows NO player: hover audio is off, the floating switch is in the
      // corner, and that is the whole of the chrome. The bar appears on exactly
      // two acts — turning hover audio ON, or pressing ▶ on a cover — and never
      // before one of them. A player at rest on a page where nothing is playing
      // is furniture, and on a grid it also steals the row it covers.
      // THE CURATOR MAP NEVER HAS A TRANSPORT (Tom, 2026-09-03): hover audio there
      // runs through its own On/Off switch and the site's sound. No bar, ever.
      // Every RESTING surface — the grid, the walls, the artist page, ATH's
      // releases / release / track / artist pages, the landings — shows a bar only
      // once something has played. The star view always has one.
      // ── THE HOVER-AUDIO TRANSPORT RULE (2026-09-04, ruled with the build) ─
      // Written here so a future drop cannot regress it by guessing:
      //
      //   THE LANDING CARDS — the idea card, the curator map and the drops
      //   card — PLAY ON HOVER AND DO NOT RAISE THE TRANSPORT. Nothing is
      //   disturbed: you move across a composition and it sounds. The state
      //   that matters on the drops card is shown in the tracklist row
      //   instead (the row glyph reads ❙❙ and takes the gold wash).
      //
      //   THE VOLUMES / RELEASES PAGE IS THE ONE THAT DOES RAISE IT. It is a
      //   list you work through rather than a composition you sweep, so the
      //   bar is the right chrome: it names what is playing and gives you the
      //   scrubber and the queue.
      //
      // Hover audio's own default is hoverArmed()'s to answer, not this key's.
      hasTransport: hasTransportNow,
      // ── AN ATH PLAY CONTROL PLAYS THIS PAGE'S RECORD (2026-09-04, r5 ask
      // 3) ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      // This called play() with no argument, so on every ATH page it resumed
      // whatever the transport happened to hold — usually the head of the
      // catalogue — rather than the drop or track on screen. recFor(name) is
      // the file's own resolver and is what the hover path already uses.
      // One resolver (pageSubject) for the action and the label alike. play()
      // already handles the pause half — it returns to paused when asked for
      // the thing already sounding — so this stays a single call.
      startPlayback: () => {
        const subject = this.pageSubject(route, st, athArtWho);
        if (subject.rec != null) { this.play(subject.rec, subject.track); return; }
        // Unnamed subject: play() would resume `cur`, and when cur is null too
        // that re-enters the playing state instead of leaving it — so the
        // control showed Pause and stayed there. The pause half is explicit
        // here, which makes it two-way in exactly the case where the record
        // could not be named. Same test the label reads, so they agree.
        if (this.subjectPlaying(subject, st)) { this.setState({ paused: true }); return; }
        this.play();
      },
      // ── THE ATH CONTROL NOW SAYS WHAT IT IS DOING (2026-09-04) ────────────
      // Same two states, same glyphs and same word as the album page's
      // recPlayLabel, from the same test — only the noun differs, because the
      // release page is about a track and the album page about a release.
      athRelPlayLabel: (() => {
        const subject = this.pageSubject(route, st, athArtWho);
        return this.subjectPlaying(subject, st) ? '❙❙ Pause' : '▶\uFE0E Play the track';
      })(),
      athArtPlayLabel: (() => {
        const subject = this.pageSubject(route, st, athArtWho);
        return this.subjectPlaying(subject, st) ? '❙❙ Pause' : '▶\uFE0E Play their track';
      })(),
      // Pulled up, the map's window is the WHOLE frame: no header above it and
      // no bottom margin below it, which is what was shrinking the scale.
      phoneMapWrapStyle: (st.sheet || 'half') === 'full'
        ? { position: 'absolute', top: 0, left: 0, right: 0, bottom: 0, zIndex: 11, overflow: 'hidden' }
        // Half-open it is a CARD in the deck like any other, not an overlay
        // pinned across the pane — that is what let it cover the cards either
        // side of it and swallow the scroll.
        : { position: 'relative', width: '100%', height: '100%', flexShrink: 0, scrollSnapAlign: 'start', scrollSnapStop: 'always', overflow: 'hidden' },
      // The deck is SHARED: the idea, the core idea, the drops and the rest are
      // the same seven cards at every width, and the map card sits third.
      //
      // ⚠ THE PHONE HALF IS UNREACHABLE SINCE 2026-09-14. This used to read
      // "only the map card differs — phone gets the drawer version, desktop
      // the full stage", true while a phone could BE on ath-map. All six doors
      // to that route now redirect a phone to ath-all-releases
      // (phoneMapPatch), so isPhoneMap cannot be true and the drawer version
      // cannot mount. Both keys still compute honestly rather than being
      // pinned false: if the map returns to the phone, nothing here needs
      // editing.
      isAthDeck: route === 'ath-map',
      isPhoneMap: narrow && route === 'ath-map',
      isDeskMap: !narrow && route === 'ath-map',
      isAth: isAth,
      athBig: athBig,
      // The content pane starts under whichever header this route wears: the
      // 106px stacked lockup on the landing, the standard 46px bar elsewhere.
      // Previously fixed at 130px, which left a header-sized mask of dead space
      // on every page that had moved to the short bar.
      // ── The curator map as an app canvas ────────────────────────────────
      // Authored at 1180 (1132 × 582 of usable space) and then
      // scaled to FIT the window: the same composition at every viewport, just
      // bigger — no scrolling, nothing cut off, centred in what is left between
      // the header and the floating transport.
      // One PANEL in a snapping scroll: the map holds a full viewport of its
      // own and locks there — scrolling past the bottom flips to the next
      // panel rather than sliding the map out of frame.
      // ── The map route as a deck of locked panels ────────────────────────
      // Six full-viewport ideas that snap into place — the idea, the core idea,
      // the map, the drops, the universe, the journal — with a bottom arrow
      // naming whatever comes next.
      athPaneRef: (el) => { this._athPane = el; },
      athIsDeck: (this._isDeck = route === 'ath-map'),
      // The deck plus the card-shaped ATH routes. Kept next to _isDeck so the
      // two can never disagree about which surfaces are app screens.
      athFitRoute: (this._fitRoute = route === 'ath-map' || route === 'ath-broadcast' || route === 'ath-subscribe'),
      // The legend is a FILTER: click a role to hold just those faces lit, click
      // the same role again to release it.
      toggleCurators: (e) => { if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation(); this.setState(s2 => ({ roleFilter: s2.roleFilter === 'c' ? null : 'c' })); },
      toggleInvited: (e) => { if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation(); this.setState(s2 => ({ roleFilter: s2.roleFilter === 'a' ? null : 'a' })); },
      mapKeyStyle: {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '20px', flexWrap: 'wrap', justifyContent: 'flex-end', flexShrink: 0, paddingBottom: '5px',
        opacity: st.sel ? 0 : 1, pointerEvents: st.sel ? 'none' : 'auto', transition: 'opacity 220ms ease'
      },
      legendCurStyle: (() => {
        const on = st.roleFilter === 'c';
        return {

          textTransform: 'uppercase', color: on ? '#b4762a' : 'var(--ink55)',

      })(),

        const on = st.roleFilter === 'a';

          textTransform: 'uppercase', color: on ? '#2f8f86' : 'var(--ink55)',
          transition: 'background 160ms ease, color 160ms ease'
        };
      })(),
      onAthPaneScroll: (e) => {
        // Deck mode positions cards absolutely and pins scrollTop at 0, so a
        // derived index is always 0 and would fight nextPanel. Ignore it there.
        if (this._isDeck) return;
        const el = e && e.currentTarget;
        if (!el || !el.clientHeight) return;
        // The scroll event is also how the pane hands us its element — the ref
        // attribute never attaches on this node, so this is the reliable handle.
        this._athPane = el;
        const idx = Math.round(el.scrollTop / el.clientHeight);
        if (idx !== this.state.panel) this.setState({ panel: idx });
      },
      deckCount: 7,
      // ── SCROLLING OFF THE END LEAVES THE DECK (2026-09-05, Tom) ───────────
      // The whole landing is scroll-scroll-scroll, and then the last card was
      // click-only: its arrow went to Volumes but the wheel clamped at 6 and
      // did nothing. One gesture for the whole page, so a down-wheel on the
      // last card does what its arrow does. It goes through nextPanel so the
      // wheel and the arrow can never disagree about the destination.
      onDeckWheel: (e) => {
        if (route !== 'ath-map') return;
        const now = Date.now();
        if (this._deckLock && now - this._deckLock < 620) return;
        const dy = e && (e.deltaY || 0);
        if (Math.abs(dy) < 8) return;
        const cur = this.state.panel || 0;
        const last = ((this._athPane && this._athPane.children.length) || 7) - 1;
        if (dy > 0 && cur >= last) { this._deckLock = now; this.goRoute('ath-all-releases'); return; }
        this._deckLock = now;
        const next = Math.min(last, Math.max(0, cur + (dy > 0 ? 1 : -1)));
        if (next !== cur) this.setState({ panel: next });
      },
      prevPanel: () => this.setState({ panel: Math.max(0, (this.state.panel || 0) - 1) }),
      // ── THE LAST CARD LEAVES, IT DOES NOT LOOP (2026-09-04, Tom) ──────────
      // The deck wrapped: Subscribe's next control read "The idea" and sent you
      // back to card 0, so the landing was a closed circuit with no way out of
      // its own bottom. A visitor who has read the whole series pitch and the
      // subscribe offer wants the catalogue, so the last card's next goes to
      // the Volumes page. Its label says Volumes for the same reason — the
      // control names its destination, and naming card 0 there was both a loop
      // and a wrong promise.
      nextPanel: () => {
        const cur = this.state.panel || 0;
        const n = (this._athPane && this._athPane.children.length) || 7;
        if (cur >= n - 1) { this.goRoute('ath-all-releases'); return; }
        this.setState({ panel: cur + 1 });
      },
      nextPanelUnused: (e) => {
        // Resolve the pane at click time: cached handle first, then the stage
        // the arrow itself lives in, then anywhere on the page.
        let el = this._athPane;
        if (!el || !el.isConnected) {
          const root = e && e.currentTarget && e.currentTarget.closest('[data-theme]');
          el = (root && root.querySelector('.sa-scroll')) || document.querySelector('.sa-scroll');
          this._athPane = el;
        }
        if (!el || !el.clientHeight) return;
        const idx = Math.round(el.scrollTop / el.clientHeight);
        const next = (idx + 1) % 7;
        el.scrollTo({ top: next * el.clientHeight, behavior: 'smooth' });
      },
      // The control names where it GOES, not where it is. The last entry was
      // 'The idea' — card 0 — because the deck looped; it is Volumes now, and
      // the handler agrees (nextPanel).
      panelNavLabel: (() => {
        const names = ['The core idea', 'The curator map', 'The drops', 'Broadcast', 'Journal', 'Subscribe', 'Volumes'];
        return names[Math.min(6, Math.max(0, st.panel || 0))];
      })(),
      panelNavStyle: (narrow && route === 'ath-map' && (st.sheet || 'half') === 'full')
        ? { display: 'none' }
        : {
        // RULING 9 — NOT FULL WIDTH. This was left:0/right:0, which made a
        // ~31px invisible strip across the whole card and swallowed the corner
        // hover-audio switch sitting at right:24/bottom:22 (rulings 6 and 7 both
        // protect that switch, and fitPanels' z-index:2 stacking context meant
        // the switch could not win on z-index either). A labelled control gets a
        // labelled-sized hit box.

      // ── YOU CANNOT BUY A DROP (2026-09-04, Tom) ───────────────────────────
      // "Buy the double LP · Vol. Two" was wrong twice: the release is not sold
      // singly — subscribing to the series is how it is got — and the volume
      // was named by a literal that would be stale on the next volume. So the
      // menu says the two true things: subscribe, or listen where it streams.
      // The § row is a section label, not a control: the streaming links are
      // listening, not acquiring, and putting them under one heading stops the
      // menu implying you can buy from Spotify.
      getMusicRows: [
        // ── NO TAIL ON THIS ROW (2026-09-04, Tom) ─────────────────────────
        // It carried 'Every volume', which printed as EVERY VOLUME in the tail
        // column and ran straight through "Subscribe to the series": the label
        // is nowrap, the tail is flex-shrink 0, and at 272px there is no room
        // for both. The streaming rows' tails are ↗ glyphs — three characters
        // wide — which is why only this row collided.
        //
        // Removed rather than made to fit: "Subscribe to the series" already
        // says it covers the series, so the tail was repeating the label in
        // smaller type. Nothing replaces it.
        ['✦', 'Subscribe to the series', '', 1],
        ['--'],
        ['§', 'Listen elsewhere', '', 0],
        ['◎', 'Bandcamp', '↗', 0],
        ['◎', 'Spotify', '↗', 0],
        ['◎', 'Apple Music', '↗', 0],
        ['◎', 'YouTube', '↗', 0]
      ].map((g, i) => {
        const rule = g[0] === '--';
        const head = g[0] === '§';
        return {
          key: 'gm' + i,
          glyph: (rule || head) ? '' : g[0],
          label: rule ? '' : g[1],
          tail: (rule || head) ? '' : g[2],
          row: rule
            ? { height: '1px', margin: '5px 8px', background: 'var(--hair)' }
            : head
            ? { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', padding: '7px 8px 4px', boxSizing: 'border-box' }
            : { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '11px', height: '38px', padding: '0 8px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '5px', cursor: 'pointer' },
          icon: (rule || head) ? { display: 'none' } : { width: '16px', flexShrink: 0, textAlign: 'center', fontSize: '12px', color: g[3] ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink55)' },
          labelStyle: rule
            ? { display: 'none' }
            : head
            ? { flex: 1, minWidth: 0, fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8px', fontWeight: 700, letterSpacing: '0.2em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink40)' }
            // Ellipsis rather than overflow, so a longer label can never again
            // run through the tail column — it truncates, which is legible.
            : { flex: 1, minWidth: 0, fontSize: '13px', color: 'var(--ink)', fontWeight: g[3] ? 600 : 400, whiteSpace: 'nowrap', overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis' },
          // A tail with nothing in it takes no space either, so the label gets
          // the whole row rather than being squeezed by an empty column.
          tailStyle: (rule || head || !g[2]) ? { display: 'none' } : { flexShrink: 0, marginLeft: '8px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.1em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink40)' },
          click: (e) => {
            if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
            if (rule || head) return;
            // Subscribe goes to the subscribe page; the rest are the host's
            // outbound links, emitted so it can open the right one.
            if (/^Subscribe/.test(g[1])) { this.setState({ getMusic: null }); this.goAth('ath-subscribe'); return; }
            const intent = /^Bandcamp/.test(g[1]) ? 'open.bandcamp'
              : /^Spotify/.test(g[1]) ? 'open.spotify'
              : /^Apple/.test(g[1]) ? 'open.apple'
              : /^YouTube/.test(g[1]) ? 'open.youtube' : null;
            if (intent) this.emit(intent, { source: 'athGetMusic' });
            this.setState({ getMusic: null });
          }
        };
      }),

      // ── The ATH artist page ───────────────────────────────────────────────
      // A mirror of the label artist page, but an ATH artist is defined by the
      // drop that invited them — so the page carries the drop instead of a
      // discography. No "records here" section: almost every one of these has
      // exactly one release on the series, and a one-row list is not a section.
      isAthArtist: route === 'ath-artist',
      // An ATH artist is one who appears in VOLS. Arriving here from anywhere
      // that left a LABEL artist in state — the page list, a label page — used to
      // render the whole template in its placeholder state, which is exactly the
      // page nobody could see. Fall back to the first real roster name instead.
      athArtName: athArtWho,
      athArtBack: () => this.goRoute('ath-artists'),
      athArtGridStyle: narrow
        ? { marginTop: '6px', display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: '22px' }
        // ONE stacked sidebar column at every desktop width. Splitting it in two
        // past 1560 made sense when the left column ran the whole page; now that
        // On tour and Video sit below, the left column is head-only and a short
        // two-across sidebar leaves a bigger hole than a tall stacked one.
        : { marginTop: '8px', display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: 'minmax(0, 1fr) 320px', columnGap: '44px', alignItems: 'start' },
      // NO CROP. A fixed height forced a crop on every photograph that was not
      // exactly that ratio — and cropping press shots causes exactly the trouble
      // it sounds like. The frame takes the photograph's OWN measured ratio, so
      // 'cover' has nothing left to cut. Landscape is preferred for this page;
      // most ATH files are square today, and a square frame is what making do
      // looks like until real landscape shots land.
      athArtPhotoStyle: (() => {
        const src = athArtPhoto, has = athArtHasPhoto, ar = athArtPhotoAR;
        // NEVER CROPPED, NEVER STRETCHED. `maxHeight` was the bug: at 16" and 20"
        // the column is wide enough that width:100% × the real ratio exceeded the
        // cap, the cap won, the box stopped matching the ratio, and `cover` cut
        // the difference off. Cap the WIDTH instead — maxH × ratio — so the box
        // always keeps the photograph's own proportions and there is nothing to
        // crop at any width.
        // Bigger on bigger screens: this is the page's hero image, and a larger
        // one also closes some of the space beside it. The RATIO is untouched —
        // only the cap moves — so it still never crops or stretches.
        const maxH = narrow ? 340 : (W >= 1900 ? 620 : W >= 1560 ? 545 : 470);
        return {
          position: 'relative',
          width: '100%', maxWidth: Math.round(maxH * this.arOf(ar)) + 'px', aspectRatio: String(this.arOf(ar)),
          borderRadius: '9px', boxSizing: 'border-box', border: '1px solid var(--hair20)',
          // No photo yet gets the MOSAIC's treatment — diagonal stripes, not a
          // flat tile — so it reads as pending rather than broken.
          background: has
            ? ('url(' + this.res(src) + ') center center/cover no-repeat, var(--tile)')
            : 'repeating-linear-gradient(135deg, var(--tile) 0 10px, transparent 10px 20px)'
        };
      })(),
      // The caption reads the SAME resolution the plate does — see athArtPhoto
      // above. It used to re-ask the question in its own IIFE off the INDEX row,
      // which is why fixing the plate alone would have stamped NO PHOTO YET
      // across 47 real photographs.
      athArtPhotoNoteStyle: (() => {
        return athArtHasPhoto
          ? { display: 'none' }
          : {
              position: 'absolute', right: '12px', top: '11px',
              fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.16em',
              textTransform: 'uppercase', fontStyle: 'italic', color: 'var(--ink40)'

        return ([d.curator].concat(d.guests || []))[i] || 'Artist pending';
      })(),
      athTrkRole: (st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx) === 0 ? 'Curator of this drop' : 'Invited to this drop',
      // ── THE SURFACE LEDGER 10 MISSED (2026-09-04, ledger 29) ──────────────
      // Three assertions in one line: a year derived from the volume index, a
      // typed "length pending", and the LP · CS · DL literal that was replaced
      // everywhere else. The year comes from the drop's own date where
      // ATH_DROPS carries one; the length from the track's duration; the
      // formats from BUY. Each part omitted with its separator when absent.
      athTrkMeta: (() => {
        const d = this.athDrop(st).r, i = st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx;
        const row = this.athDropRow('Vol. ' + (d.vi + 1) + ' Drop ' + d.num);
        const parts = [];
        const year = (() => {
          const m = String((row && row.meta) || '').match(/(19|20)\d{2}/);
          return m ? m[0] : '';
        })();
        if (year) parts.push(year);
        const trk = this.athDropTrack('Vol. ' + (d.vi + 1) + ' Drop ' + d.num, i);
        if (trk && trk[5]) parts.push(trk[5]);
        const formats = (this.data('BUY') || []).map(b => b[0]).filter((v, k, a) => v && a.indexOf(v) === k);
        if (formats.length) parts.push(formats.join(' · '));
        return parts.join(' · ');
      })(),
      athTrkGoArtist: () => {
        const d = this.athDrop(st).r, i = st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx;
        const n = ([d.curator].concat(d.guests || []))[i];
        // Tom's case on ATH: from a track to its artist, without closing the map.
        if (n) this.setState(this.artistPatch(n));
      },
      // The curator's note is ABOUT the drop; a track carries the artist's own
      // line about this recording. Only the curator's track of a drop whose
      // words exist has one, so the rest say so instead of inventing it.
      // "On this track" for both roles: the mark names the block, and the body
      // says whose note is pending, so the two can never disagree.
      athTrkQuoteMark: 'On this track',
      // A drop's note is the curator writing about the OTHER two tracks; it is
      // not a note about any single recording, so it stays on the release page
      // and is never reused here. Per-track notes have no copy yet — the whole
      // file's convention is to say so rather than borrow the nearest text.
      // The track's own artist first: if THEY have written words, this is the
      // page to show them on (2026-09-05). Only then the pending line, which
      // is worded for whose note is missing.
      athTrkQuote: (() => {
        const d = this.athDrop(st).r, i = st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx;
        const who = ([d.curator].concat(d.guests || []))[i];
        const own = this.athStatementFor(who);
        if (own) return own;
        return i === 0
          ? 'The curator’s note on this recording is pending — one paragraph, in their own words, on what they made for this drop.'
          : 'The artist’s note on this recording is pending — one paragraph, in their own words, on what they sent in and why.';
      })(),
      athTrkQuoteAttr: (() => {
        const d = this.athDrop(st).r, i = st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx;
        const who = ([d.curator].concat(d.guests || []))[i];
        return this.athStatementFor(who) ? who : '';
      })(),
      // A REAL quote wears the feature's dress — solid tint, gold rule, ink at
      // full strength; a pending one keeps the dashed outline and muted italic
      // so the two are never mistaken for each other (2026-09-05).
      athTrkQuoteStyle: (() => {
        const d = this.athDrop(st).r, i = st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx;
        const real = !!this.athStatementFor(([d.curator].concat(d.guests || []))[i]);
        return {
          marginTop: narrow ? '20px' : '26px', padding: narrow ? '15px 17px' : '20px 24px',
          boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '7px',
          background: real ? 'rgba(138,106,18,0.07)' : 'transparent',
          border: real ? 'none' : '1px dashed var(--hair20)',
          borderTop: real ? '2px solid var(--gold)' : '1px dashed var(--hair20)'
        };
      })(),
      athTrkQuoteBodyStyle: (() => {
        const d = this.athDrop(st).r, i = st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx;

        return {
          marginTop: '10px', fontSize: narrow ? '14px' : '16px', lineHeight: 1.6,

          fontStyle: real ? 'normal' : 'italic', textWrap: 'pretty'
        };
      })(),
      athTrkBio: (() => {
        const d = this.athDrop(st).r, i = st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx;

        const known = n ? this.data('INDEX').filter(x => x[0] === n)[0] : null;
        return this.artistBio(n || '') || 'Biography pending — the artist page carries the full note, discography and press shots.';
      })(),
      athTrkArtBioStyle: (() => {
        const d = this.athDrop(st).r, i = st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx;

        return { display: 'block', marginTop: '8px', fontSize: '12.5px', lineHeight: 1.6, color: has ? 'var(--ink70)' : 'var(--ink40)', fontStyle: has ? 'normal' : 'italic', textWrap: 'pretty' };

        const d = this.athDrop(st).r, i = st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx;

          play: (e) => {
            if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();

            // ⚠ NO RECORD MEANS NO PLAYBACK, not play() with no argument.
            // `else this.play()` set started: true with npPlay left null, and

            if (rec != null) this.play(rec, x.si);

          name: x.n,
          titleStyle: { display: 'block', marginTop: '2px', fontSize: '12px', color: 'var(--ink55)', fontStyle: x.si === 0 ? 'normal' : 'italic' },
          title: x.si === 0 ? d.track : 'Title pending',
          // ⚠ THE GLYPH KEY, WHICH I POINTED THE MARKUP AT WITHOUT ADDING IT.
          // Template line 1713 read a literal ▶︎; I changed it to
          // {{ s.playGlyph }} on the assumption this was the radio loop, and it

          //
          // Not reverted to a literal, which is what stopped the strand button

          playGlyph: (() => {
            const rec = this.recFor(x.n) != null ? this.recFor(x.n) : this.recFor(d.curator);

              && rec != null && st.npPlay === rec
              && (st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx) === x.si;
            return on ? '❙❙' : '▶︎';

          // Absent where there is no record to play, same rule as the curator

          ),
          dotsStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', width: '30px', height: '30px', flexShrink: 0, boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '4px', fontSize: '14px', color: 'var(--ink70)', cursor: 'pointer' },
          dots: this.openCtx('track', null),
          click: () => this.setState({ route: 'ath-track', trackIdx: x.si, getMusic: null })
        }));
      })(),

      // ── About this release / About this track ─────────────────────────────
      // Bandcamp's own description field, carried through verbatim. It is where

      athAboutBodyStyle: (() => {

      // The grid cards were already clickable but said nothing about it, so the

      // ── The curator map · four layouts, one renderer ─────────────────────
