      drawTools: [
        ['Cancel', 'cancel', !!st.linkFrom, 'Drop the face you just picked'],
        ['Undo last', 'undo', (st.links || []).length > 0, 'Remove the connection you drew most recently'],
        ['Clear all', 'clear', (st.links || []).length > 0, 'Remove every connection you have drawn'],
        ['Done', 'done', !!st.draw, 'Leave drawing mode']
      ].map((t, i) => ({
        key: 'dt' + i,
        label: t[0], title: t[3],
        box: {
          display: t[2] ? 'flex' : 'none', alignItems: 'center', height: '30px', padding: '0 11px',
          boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '5px', cursor: 'pointer', whiteSpace: 'nowrap',
          border: '1px solid ' + (t[1] === 'clear' ? 'rgba(180,60,40,0.4)' : 'var(--hair20)'),
          color: t[1] === 'clear' ? 'rgb(150,52,34)' : 'var(--ink70)',
          fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.12em', textTransform: 'uppercase'
        },
        click: (e) => {
          if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
          if (t[1] === 'cancel') this.setState({ linkFrom: null });
          else if (t[1] === 'undo') this.removeLink((this.state.links || []).length - 1);
          else if (t[1] === 'clear') { this.saveLinks([]); this.setState({ links: [], linkFrom: null }); }
          else this.setState({ draw: false, linkFrom: null });
        }
      })),
      // linkCount removed 2026-09-04 (COUNT-INVENTORY #31): a development
      // readout of how many links the layout drew, printed on the curator map.
      // Not content, and nobody outside the studio wants it. hasLinks stays —
      // it is a real condition the layout reads.
      hasLinks: (st.links || []).length > 0,
      linkChips: (st.links || []).map((l, i) => ({
        key: 'lk' + i,
        label: (i + 1) + ' · ' + l[0] + ' → ' + l[1],
        box: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '8px', height: '28px', padding: '0 10px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '4px', background: 'rgba(138,106,18,0.12)', border: '1px solid rgba(138,106,18,0.35)', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.06em', color: 'var(--gold)', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' },
        x: { cursor: 'pointer', color: 'var(--ink55)', fontSize: '10px' },
        remove: (e) => { if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation(); this.removeLink(i); }
      })),

      // ── THE GLOBAL SHOW-LINKS TOGGLE IS RETIRED (DQ-36, 2026-09-01) ───────
      // linksToggleStyle / toggleLinks / linksToggleLabel are DELETED. Third
      // instance of the pattern conArrayStyle and clearConstellation were the
      // first two: a control defined here, drawn by no markup, and read by the
      // build as intent. The build was right that it has no such control, and
      // r2j §1 was wrong to name it as a target for the tint.
      //
      // The curator map's behaviour is the one in linksNote below and it stands:
      // click a face for its drop's connections, hover an oval to read it. A
      // global "show all 53" was an earlier idea — 53 ovals at once is a web,
      // not a map — and it is not coming back. showLinks state stays: the
      // per-face selection uses it.
      linksNote: 'Click a face for its drop\u2019s connections · hover an oval to read it',

      // ── The pill panel ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
      // Clicking a drop lists its three artists; each carries a pill per
      // documented connection, and the pill IS the story. Hovering one lights
      // the two artists it joins across both volumes and prints the full text
      // below, so the names and the sentence can be read together. No lines.
      // ── The connections field ────────────────────────────────────────────
      //   · FIXED height, always: three ranks maximum, so the panel is the same
      //     size whether an artist has two connections or twenty, and Volume Two
      //     never moves. A busier artist packs tighter, it does not push down.
      //   · Bigger margin above (from Volume One), smaller below (to Volume Two).
      //   · The clump is ORGANIC: each dot carries its own offset from a fixed
      //     hash of its index, so it never reads as a grid.
      //   · One oval at a time, parked in the empty margin at the FAR LEFT of
      //     the whole panel — same place for every dot in every group, so it is
      //     steady and readable while both portraits stay lit.
      pillPanelStyle: (() => {
        const open = !!(st.selTrio && st.selTrio.length);
        // TWO heights only — closed and open. Nothing about the chosen drop
        // affects it, so Volume Two sits still while you click through drops.
        // 26 above · title 11 · 14 gap · names 11 · 8 gap · 2 ranks 40 · 4 below
        return {
          position: 'relative', height: open ? '82px' : '18px',
          margin: 0, padding: 0, boxSizing: 'border-box', overflow: 'visible',
          // Three equal columns: left lane · the names · right lane.
          display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: '1fr 1fr 1fr', gridTemplateRows: 'auto auto', alignItems: 'start',
          // ⚠ ONE transition, WAS TWO (2026-09-14 audit). This literal declared
          // `height 320ms` above and `height 340ms` here. Both were live in the
          // same object, so the later silently won and the 320 was dead — two
          // intentions, one invisible, and no way for a reader to tell which
          // the design meant. 340 is kept because 340 is what has been
          // shipping; the 320 is deleted rather than averaged, since a
          // duplicate key is never a decision.
          transition: 'height 340ms cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1)'
        };
      })(),
      pillDividerStyle: { display: 'none' },
      pillHead: '',
      // 38px above the title, 17px below the last rank — the block sits low in
      // its gap, close to Volume Two, per the note.
      pillHeadStyle: false
        ? { gridColumn: '1 / -1', gridRow: '1', display: 'block', textAlign: 'center', paddingTop: '22px',
            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8.5px', fontWeight: 700,
            letterSpacing: '0.24em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--gold)', pointerEvents: 'none' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      // Column two of the grid: the three artists, centred in it.
      pillRowsStyle: (st.selTrio && st.selTrio.length)
        ? { gridColumn: '2', gridRow: '2', display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'row', justifyContent: 'center',
            alignItems: 'flex-start', gap: '46px', paddingTop: '38px' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      // The oval's lane: the empty margin at the far left of the panel, one
      // position for every dot so it never jumps or flickers between hovers.
      ovalText: (() => {
        const hc = st.pillHover != null ? this.data('CONNECTIONS')[st.pillHover] : null;
        return hc ? hc[3] : '';
      })(),
      ovalStyle: (() => {
        const hc = st.pillHover != null ? this.data('CONNECTIONS')[st.pillHover] : null;
        if (!hc || !(st.selTrio && st.selTrio.length)) return { display: 'none' };
        const words = hc[3].split(' ').length;
        // Side is decided by where the SELECTED DROP sits, not by which group
        // owns the dot: the magnified cell is large now, and the quote has to
        // land clear of it.
        const dropRight = (st.selDi != null ? st.selDi : 0) >= 4;
        // Which group owns the hovered dot decides the side: the curator (first)
        // opens in the left margin, the two invited artists in the right.
        const trio = (st.selTrio || []).map(n => this.slug(n));
        let owner = 0;
        for (let g = 0; g < trio.length; g++) {
          const list = [];
          this.data('CONNECTIONS').forEach((cn, ci) => {
            const mem = [cn[0], cn[1], cn[2]].filter(Boolean).map(n => this.slug(String(n).split(':')[0]));
            if (mem.indexOf(trio[g]) < 0) return;
            // One dot per note: the earliest trio member in it owns the dot.
            const owners = mem.map(m => trio.indexOf(m)).filter(i => i > -1);
            if (owners.length && Math.min.apply(null, owners) < g) return;
            list.push(ci);
          });
          if (list.indexOf(st.pillHover) > -1) { owner = g; break; }
        }
        // THE CURATOR'S DOT OPENS LEFT, an invited artist's opens right — the
        // curator is the first of the trio, the two invitees the second and
        // third. `owner` was already being derived for exactly this and then
        // thrown away, so the side had reverted to the drop's own position.
        // Phone is a different layout entirely and keeps its own rule.
        const quoteLeft = narrow ? dropRight : (owner === 0);
        return {
          // Centred on the CONTENT block (names + dots), not on the panel box:
          // the panel's top 34px is the gap under Volume One, so centring in the
          // panel put the quote above the names. This region runs from the names'
          // top edge to the bottom of the clump.
          // The box fills its whole lane via insets — no width/maxWidth on it,
          // because a maxWidth on a left-anchored absolute box pins the text to
          // the lane's left edge instead of centring it. The inner span carries
          // the reading width.
          position: 'absolute', top: '34px', bottom: '0px',
          left: quoteLeft ? '0' : '66.667%', right: quoteLeft ? '66.667%' : '0',
          display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center',
          zIndex: 60,
          // No shape at all now: bare text on the white field, sitting centred
          // in its empty lane. Width still caps so it wraps instead of running
          // into the names; nothing behind it, no padding, no radius.
          pointerEvents: 'none'
        };
      })(),
      ovalTextStyle: (() => {
        const hc = st.pillHover != null ? this.data('CONNECTIONS')[st.pillHover] : null;
        if (!hc) return { display: 'none' };
        const words = hc[3].split(' ').length;
        return {
          display: 'block', maxWidth: '300px',
          color: st.pillInk || '#c9a545',
          fontSize: (words > 34 ? '10px' : '11px'),
          fontStyle: 'italic', lineHeight: 1.55,
          textAlign: 'center', textWrap: 'pretty'
        };
      })(),
      // The old "mouse over to see connections" sat UNDER the dots in ink40 and
      // read as a caption nobody looks at. It is gone; the cue below does the
      // job instead — outside the field so it never covers a pop-up, pointing
      // INTO it, and only while a drop is actually open.
      pillHint: '',
      pillHintStyle: { display: 'none' },
      exploreCueStyle: (st.selTrio && st.selTrio.length && st.pillHover == null)
        ? {
            // ALL the way left, in the margin: it was justified to the END of
            // column 1, which put it right where the connection names begin.
            // Now it starts at the column's left edge, clear of the map.
            // Centred on the WHOLE band between the two volumes — it stretches
            // the row and centres inside it, rather than sitting at the top of
            // its own content box.
            gridColumn: '1', gridRow: '2', alignSelf: 'stretch', justifySelf: 'start',
            display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', alignItems: 'flex-start',
            justifyContent: 'center', gap: '6px',
            paddingTop: '34px', paddingLeft: '2px', whiteSpace: 'nowrap', pointerEvents: 'none',
            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '11px',

        // and the connection copy it was pointing at now needs the room.
        : { display: 'none' },
      exploreCueArrowStyle: {
        fontSize: '22px', lineHeight: 1, color: 'var(--gold)',
        // INFINITE, not twice: the arrow is the part that says "go and mess with
        // this", so it keeps moving. The label settles.
        animation: 'sa-cue-arrow 1.15s ease-in-out 420ms infinite'
      },
      pillGroups: (st.selTrio || []).map((who, gi) => {
        const trio = (st.selTrio || []).map(n => this.slug(n));
        // gi is kept as the ownership index even after empty rows drop out.
        const mine = [];
        this.data('CONNECTIONS').forEach((cn, ci) => {
          const raw = [cn[0], cn[1], cn[2]].filter(Boolean).map(n => String(n).split(':')[0]);
          const mem = raw.map(n => this.slug(n));
          if (mem.indexOf(this.slug(who)) < 0) return;
          const owners = mem.map(m => trio.indexOf(m)).filter(i => i > -1);
          if (owners.length && Math.min.apply(null, owners) < gi) return;
          const others = raw.filter(n => this.slug(n) !== this.slug(who));
          mine.push({ ci: ci, other: others.join(' · '), full: cn[3] });
        });
        // The lightest end of the spectrum: pale butter, sky, mint, blush.
        const PASTEL = ['#f6ecc8', '#dbeaf4', '#dcefe4', '#f7e6d8', '#e6e9f5', '#f5e3ea'];
        // Same hues carried to a legible weight for the quote text on white.
        const PASTEL_INK = ['#c9a545', '#7aa8c6', '#7cb69b', '#d09a6f', '#9a9fcb', '#cf94a9'];
        // Two ranks, so a clump is wide and shallow rather than a block.
        // ONE row, not ranks: a single jagged line of dots reads as a cluster
        // and gives the margin below it back.
        const nDots = Math.max(1, mine.length);
        const pitch = nDots > 8 ? 20 : (nDots > 4 ? 22 : 17);
        const runW = nDots * pitch;
        return {
          key: 'pg' + gi,
          name: who,
          rowStyle: { position: 'relative', display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', alignItems: 'center', gap: '16px', minWidth: 0 },
          nameStyle: { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8.5px', letterSpacing: '0.18em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)', whiteSpace: 'nowrap', textAlign: 'center' },
          emptyStyle: { display: 'none' },
          // Fixed two-rank box, so no clump can change the panel's height.
          clumpStyle: { position: 'relative', height: '26px', width: runW + 'px', display: 'block' },
          pills: mine.map((m, pi) => {
            const hot = st.pillHover === m.ci;
            const colour = PASTEL[pi % PASTEL.length];
            // Organic placement: rank and file from the index, then a small
            // deterministic wobble so no two dots line up.
            const rank = 0, file = pi;
            // How many dots share this rank — used to centre the rank inside
            // the clump, so every rank is centred under the artist's name.

            // Deterministic jitter on both axes, and a size that varies per dot,
            // so the clump reads as cells rather than a grid — and rows are
            // nudged off each other's baseline.
            const h1 = ((pi * 2654435761) % 97) / 97 - 0.5;
            const h2 = ((pi * 40503 + 17) % 89) / 89 - 0.5;
            const h3 = ((pi * 7919 + 41) % 71) / 71;
            const h4 = ((pi * 104729 + 7) % 83) / 83;
            // 10–26px, and each dot's own step, so the gaps are uneven too.
            const d = 10 + Math.round(h3 * 16);
            // A small per-dot wobble around the even pitch keeps the row jagged.
            const wob = Math.round((h4 - 0.5) * pitch * 0.3);
            return {
              key: 'pl' + m.ci,
              label: '',
              full: m.full,
              box: {
                position: 'absolute',
                left: Math.round(file * pitch + wob + h1 * 5) + 'px',
                top: Math.round(rank * 20 + h2 * 13) + 'px',
                width: d + 'px', height: d + 'px', borderRadius: '50%',
                background: colour, opacity: hot ? 1 : 0.92,
                transform: hot ? 'scale(2.1)' : 'none',
                // ── THE HOVERED DOT KEEPS THE POINTER (2026-09-10, Tom: "it
                // does a really crazy flashing… sometimes it goes between two
                // different texts and flashes back and forth") ───────────────
                // Two dots trading the hover, not one dot flickering. These
                // are 10–26px on a jittered pitch, and a hovered one scales
                // 2.1× — at the DRAWER's compressed width that enlarged disc
                // reaches over its neighbours. With every dot at the same
                // z-level the LATER sibling paints on top, so the pointer
                // sitting in the overlap enters the neighbour, pillHover
                // switches, this dot shrinks, the pointer is back over this
                // one — and the sentence above rewrites on every exchange.
                // That is the shake, and it is why the full-width desktop map
                // is fine: its pitch is wide enough that a scaled dot reaches
                // nothing.
                //
                // The phone solved the same class with a counter-scaled 44px
                // pad — "the target under the cursor cannot change without the
                // cursor moving" — and pairs it with zIndex. That pad needs an
                // inner element this builder does not have; the z-order half
                // is sufficient on its own here, because a dot that owns the
                // top of the stack cannot lose the pointer to something
                // underneath it. Entry is always into the RESTING disc and the
                // scale only grows from the centre, so the pointer it captured
                // stays captured until it is moved out.
                zIndex: hot ? 5 : 1,
                cursor: 'default', transition: 'transform 160ms ease, opacity 160ms ease'
              },
              enter: () => this.hoverEnter({ pillHover: m.ci, pillColour: colour, pillInk: PASTEL_INK[pi % PASTEL_INK.length] }),
              leave: () => this.setState({ pillHover: null })
            };
          })
        };
      }).filter(g => g.pills.length > 0),
      pillReadStyle: { display: 'none' },

      selKicker: (st.hoverLink != null || st.selLink != null) ? 'Connection' : st.sel ? (st.selDrop || 'Selected') : 'Select a name',
      selName: st.sel || 'Nothing selected',
      selBody: st.sel
        ? (st.selKind === 'c'
            ? 'Curator — contributed “' + (st.selTrack || '') + '”, then introduced the two artists beneath.'
              + (this.laterCurator(st.sel) ? '' : '')
            : 'Invited by ' + (st.selCurator || '') + ' into “' + (st.selDrop || '') + '”.'
              + (this.laterCurator(st.sel) ? ' Later curates a drop of their own — that crossing is the whole idea.' : '')
              + (st.selVerified ? '' : ' This pairing is read from the reference map and is not confirmed by the curator pages yet.'))
        : 'Click any face to trace its connections — the rest of the map dims and the “introduced by” lines light up. Forty-six of forty-eight names are published; Volume Two Drop 8 has no guests announced yet.',
      selBodyStyle: { marginTop: '8px', fontSize: '13.5px', lineHeight: 1.6, maxWidth: '68ch', color: st.sel ? 'var(--ink70)' : 'var(--ink40)', fontStyle: st.sel ? 'normal' : 'italic', textWrap: 'pretty' },
      selResetStyle: {
        flexShrink: 0, display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', height: '40px', padding: '0 14px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
        borderRadius: '5px', border: '1px solid var(--hair20)', cursor: 'pointer',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.12em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
        color: st.sel ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink40)'
      },
      // Any white space closes the whole thing — selection, trio, panel, oval.
      clearSel: () => this.setState({ roleFilter: null, sel: null, selLink: null, selTrio: null, mnhover: null, hoverLink: null, pillHover: null, selDrop: null, selTrack: null, selCurator: null, selKind: null }),
      // The readout sits ABOVE the map, so a selection is never off-screen.
      selBarStyle: {
        marginTop: '18px', minHeight: '58px', padding: '12px 16px', boxSizing: 'border-box',

      },
      selLineStyle: { fontSize: '14px', lineHeight: 1.45, color: (st.sel || st.selLink != null || st.hoverLink != null) ? 'var(--ink)' : 'var(--ink40)', fontStyle: (st.sel || st.selLink != null || st.hoverLink != null) ? 'normal' : 'italic', textWrap: 'pretty' },
      // The box above the chart fills on HOVER as well as click, so tracing a
      // strand reads its story live — the "tooltip" is this inline panel.
      selLine: (st.hoverLink != null || st.selLink != null) && this.data('CONNECTIONS')[st.hoverLink != null ? st.hoverLink : st.selLink]
        ? (function (cn) { return [cn[0], cn[1], cn[2]].filter(Boolean).map(n => String(n).split(':')[0]).join(' ✕ ') + ' — ' + cn[3]; })(this.data('CONNECTIONS')[st.hoverLink != null ? st.hoverLink : st.selLink])
        : st.sel
        ? (st.selKind === 'c'
            ? st.sel + ' — contributed “' + (st.selTrack || '') + '”, then introduced the two artists on this drop.'
            : st.sel + ' — invited by ' + (st.selCurator || '') + '.' + (this.laterCurator(st.sel) ? ' Later curates a drop of their own.' : ''))
        : 'Click a face to trace a connection — the rest of the map dims. Click any empty space to clear.',
      mapReadoutStyle: { marginTop: '18px', paddingTop: '18px', borderTop: '1px solid var(--hair)', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'flex-start', justifyContent: 'space-between', gap: '26px' },

      // ⚠ DEAD, AND IT WAS THE SEED OF A WRONG PAGE. NOT A SPECIFICATION.
      //
      // Nothing in this file's markup reads athArtistDrops — verified, zero
      // references. It is the BY-DROP artists layout: artists grouped by drop,
      // three across, bios beneath. That layout is RETIRED (Tom, 2026-09-02:
      // "This is not the ATH artist index, which is a mosaic"). The live
      // artists index is the mosaic, athMosaicRows.
      //
      // This block used to be captioned with frame citations — 15f for the

      // list. Those are frames of the RETIRED suite, and between this caption,
      // athOtherLabel's '(15f)' and a comment of mine claiming "the build has

      // turned out to hold something real, and deletion needs a ruling too.
      // DO NOT BUILD FROM IT. Do not re-cite it. If the by-drop view is ever

      // athArtistDrops DELETED (§58): the by-drop artists data, referenced by no markup, captioned to retired frames, and the only remaining mock-name source on the artist route.
      athOtherLabel: route === 'ath-artists' ? 'Artists · the mosaic wall, name over the photograph'
        : route === 'ath-artist' ? 'Artist page · portrait left, records beneath'
        : route === 'ath-journal' ? 'Journal · field notes, the lead article beside the list'
        : route === 'ath-about' ? 'ATH has no About page — the landing is the About'
        : 'Track page · one recording, its two drop-mates at the foot',
      // athSite, not isAth (2026-09-09, code audit part 5). The pill is DRAWN on
      // identity — subscribePillStyle tests athSite — and was HANDLED on the route
      // prefix, so on ATH's star map and catalogue, where identity is ATH and the

      goSubscribe: () => athSite ? this.goCard(6) : this.goAth('ath-subscribe'),

      // 6 (/subscribe); elsewhere it is the standalone route, which now has the
      // same address. The href and the click must name one destination.
      subscribeHref: athSite ? this.addr('ath-card', 6) : this.addr('ath-subscribe'),
      goAthJournal: () => this.goAth('ath-journal'),

      // 15b's drop rail. Drop 3 is live and real; Drop 2 carries the values the
      // frame prints. Only photographs we actually hold are shown — the rest
      // say "community photo", which is what the frame does too.
      // ── A ROW'S CONTROL BELONGS TO THE ROW (2026-09-04, DQ-43) ────────────
      // These rows carried d.playStyle — the button's LOOK — but bound the
      // top-level startPlayback and buyAlbum for its ACTION, so every row
      // played and, worse, BOUGHT drop 1. A global handler receives nothing
      // saying which row was clicked, so the host could not patch it either.
      // Each row now carries its own play and buy, resolved through the same
      // recFor() the rest of the file uses. catCards was already the model.
      athDrops: this.data('ATH_DROPS').map((d, di) => ({
        key: d.key,
        rec: (() => { const who = String(d.artists || '').split(' · ')[0]; return who ? this.recFor(who) : null; })(),
        play: (e) => {
          if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
          const who = String(d.artists || '').split(' · ')[0];
          const rec = who ? this.recFor(who) : null;
          if (rec != null) this.play(rec, 0);
        },
        buy: (e) => {
          if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
          const who = String(d.artists || '').split(' · ')[0];
          const rec = who ? this.recFor(who) : null;
          this.openBuy(rec != null ? rec : 0)(e);
        },
        gutter: { borderRight: '1px solid var(--hair)', display: 'flex', justifyContent: 'flex-end', paddingTop: di === 0 ? '2px' : '32px' },
        gutterInner: d.live
          ? { width: '66px', padding: '10px 12px 12px 0', boxSizing: 'border-box', marginRight: '-1px', borderRight: '2px solid #8a6a12', textAlign: 'right' }
          : { width: '66px', padding: '10px 12px 12px 0', boxSizing: 'border-box', textAlign: 'right' },
        dNum: d.dNum,
        dNumStyle: { display: 'block', marginTop: '4px', fontSize: '26px', fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: '-0.02em', lineHeight: 1, color: d.live ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink55)' },
        nowStyle: d.live
          ? { display: 'block', marginTop: '8px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--gold)' }
          : { display: 'none' },
        body: { paddingTop: di === 0 ? 0 : '30px', paddingBottom: '30px', borderBottom: '1px solid var(--hair)' },
        cover: {
          width: '56px', height: '56px', flexShrink: 0, borderRadius: '5px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
          border: '1px solid ' + (d.live ? 'rgba(138,106,18,0.5)' : 'var(--hair20)'),
          background: d.cover
            ? 'url(' + this.res(d.cover) + ') center/cover no-repeat'
            : 'repeating-linear-gradient(135deg, var(--tile) 0 6px, transparent 6px 12px)'
        },
        title: d.title, artists: d.artists, meta: d.meta, curator: d.curator, note: d.note,
        playStyle: this.playCtl(d.live
          ? { padding: '12px 16px', borderRadius: '5px', background: 'var(--gold)', color: 'var(--ongold)', fontWeight: 700, cursor: 'pointer', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' }
          : { padding: '12px 16px', borderRadius: '5px', border: '1px solid var(--hair20)', color: 'var(--ink70)', cursor: 'pointer', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' }),
        tracks: this.athDropTracks(d).map((t, ti) => ({
          key: 't' + ti,
          artist: t[0], track: t[1], home: t[2],
          photo: {
            width: '100%', height: '190px', borderRadius: '7px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
            border: '1px solid var(--hair20)',
            background: t[3]
              ? 'url(' + this.res(t[3]) + ') center 22%/cover no-repeat'
              : 'repeating-linear-gradient(' + (140 + ti * 10) + 'deg, var(--tile) 0 7px, transparent 7px 15px)',
            display: 'flex', alignItems: 'flex-end', padding: '12px'
          },
          photoNoteStyle: t[3] ? { display: 'none' } : { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8px', letterSpacing: '0.2em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink40)' }
        }))
      })),

      // ── Drops, newest first (frame 15e) ──────────────────────────────────
      // One row per drop: the three covers, the three tracks, the curator line.
      // The drops page shows the newest; the releases page shows every drop,
      // lazy-loaded a few at a time.
      athLatestDrop: (() => {
        // Released rows only (§ d[4]): on the landing an unreleased drop was
        // reachable here because the listing and the map share this dataset.
        const all = this.buildDrops(st).all.filter(r => r.released !== 0);
        const i = all.findIndex(r => r.title === this.newestReleased().label);
        const from = i > -1 ? i : 0;
        // The anchor is NEWEST_RELEASED — 6 through 8 are unreleased and have no
        // covers, so the card never reaches past it. ONE on a phone: two drops
        // of covers-plus-tracklist is more than a 390-wide card can hold, and
        // "See all releases" is right there for the rest.
        const n = aNarrow ? 1 : 2;
        return all.slice(from, from + n).map(r => Object.assign({}, r, this.getMusicOn(st, r.title)));
      })(),
      // ⚠ THE SAME GATE, AND THE COUNT WITH IT (2026-09-10). This listed every
      // drop while the Starmap column listed only the public ones, so the two
      // release surfaces disagreed — and total/more came from the unfiltered
      // set, which would have printed a count the rows could not account for.
      athShownDrops: this.buildDrops(st).shown.filter(r => this.athDropIsPublic(r)).map(r => Object.assign({}, r, this.getMusicOn(st, r.title))),
      athHasMoreDrops: this.buildDrops(st).all.filter(r => this.athDropIsPublic(r)).length > this.buildDrops(st).shown.filter(r => this.athDropIsPublic(r)).length,
      athDropTotal: this.buildDrops(st).all.filter(r => this.athDropIsPublic(r)).length,
      // The four adapted zine pieces. One template serves all of them: the rail
      // carries the whole set plus the latest drop, and the article sits beside
      // it in a fixed reading measure — that is what makes full width work for
      // copy that is naturally narrow and long.
      athArticles: (() => {
        const A = this.data('JOURNAL');
        const cur = Math.min(A.length - 1, Math.max(0, st.article || 0));
        return A.map((x, i) => ({
          key: x[0], kicker: x[1], title: x[2], dek: x[3], byline: x[4], img: x[5], where: x[6],
          url: 'https://acrossthehorizon.space/journal/' + x[0] + '.html',
          // ── EVERY PIECE YOU ADVERTISE IS REACHABLE (2026-09-06, Tom) ────────
          // "Make sure you can get to the articles that you're advertising."
          // The rail rows were <div onClick> — they navigated, but only on a
          // plain left click, and any row whose click did not resolve was
          // simply dead with nothing to say so. href is the fix and the proof:
          // a row either has an address or it does not, and cmd-click,
          // middle-click and "copy link address" come back with it.
          // Root-relative, like the journal cards — the .music-vs-.space
          // question is still open and a relative path is right under either.
          href: this.addr('journal', x[0]),
          current: i === cur,
          railStyle: {
            display: 'flex', gap: '12px', padding: '13px 0', cursor: 'pointer',
            borderTop: i === 0 ? 'none' : '1px solid var(--hair)',
            opacity: i === cur ? 1 : 0.62
          },
          railThumb: { width: '54px', height: '54px', flexShrink: 0, borderRadius: '4px', border: '1px solid ' + (i === cur ? 'rgba(138,106,18,0.6)' : 'var(--hair)'), background: 'url(' + this.res(x[5]) + ') center 30%/cover no-repeat, var(--tile)' },
          railTitle: { fontSize: '12.5px', fontWeight: 600, lineHeight: 1.3, color: i === cur ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink)' },
          // The drawer's own row: the rail's shape without its "current piece"
          // dimming, because in the column every article is equally available —
          // nothing is being read yet. Its own keys rather than reusing
          // railStyle, so the sidebar and the article page's rail can differ
          // where they should and cannot drift where they should not.
          drawerRow: {
            display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '12px',
            padding: '12px 22px', borderBottom: '1px solid var(--hair)',
            cursor: 'pointer', textDecoration: 'none'
          },
          drawerKicker: {
            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8px',
            letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)'
          },
          click: () => this.setState({ route: 'ath-article', article: i, browseOpen: false, explore: false })
        }));
      })(),
      // ── §1 of r47, SECOND SITE · THE JOURNAL ANCHORS ALSO UNLOADED ────────
      // ⚠ FOUND BY REPRODUCING YOUR ASK 1 RATHER THAN BY READING. You reported the
      // catalogue cover; the journal lead and its cards had the same shape — a
      // real href and a handler that never cancelled the browser — so clicking
      // one left the document. My own probe hung on it, which is how it surfaced.
      // Every anchor this file draws to its OWN surfaces now goes through
      // navTo(); the only anchors that still navigate are the ones that should
      // (Explore, other brands, tickets, socials), and they carry target/rel.
      //
      // AND A SECOND DEFECT IN THE SAME LINE: this was used directly as an
      // onClick, so `i` arrived as the click EVENT, `typeof i === 'number'` was
      // false, and the lead always opened article 0 — whatever piece the lead
      // actually was. It now resolves the lead's own index from the list.
      goAthArticle: this.navTo((i) => {
        if (typeof i === 'number') return this.setState({ route: 'ath-article', article: i, browseOpen: false, explore: false });
        const lead = jrnLead ? this.data('JOURNAL').indexOf(jrnLead) : -1;
        return this.setState({ route: 'ath-article', article: lead > -1 ? lead : 0, browseOpen: false, explore: false });
      }),

      // ── The Journal, expanded (15c) ────────────────────────────────────────
      // The deck's card is a teaser: masthead, one lead, three in a rail. The
      // ROUTE is the whole run — the same masthead, then a kind filter, the
      // lead at full width, and every remaining piece as a card. Filtering
      // re-picks the lead rather than leaving an empty hero above the grid.
      jrnPadStyle: { padding: narrow ? '22px 14px 30px' : '28px 24px 40px', boxSizing: 'border-box' },
      jrnMastStyle: narrow
        ? { display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: '18px', paddingBottom: '18px', borderBottom: '1px solid var(--hair)' }
        : { display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: '1fr 340px', gap: '40px', alignItems: 'start', paddingBottom: '20px', borderBottom: '1px solid var(--hair)' },
      jrnTitleStyle: { marginTop: '10px', fontSize: narrow ? '24px' : '34px', fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: '-0.028em', lineHeight: 1.08, color: 'var(--ink)' },
      jrnLedeStyle: { marginTop: '12px', fontSize: narrow ? '13.5px' : '15px', lineHeight: 1.6, maxWidth: '62ch', color: 'var(--ink70)', textWrap: 'pretty' },
      jrnAsideStyle: narrow
        ? { paddingLeft: '16px', borderLeft: '1px solid rgba(138,106,18,0.5)' }
        : { paddingLeft: '20px', borderLeft: '1px solid rgba(138,106,18,0.5)' },
      jrnAsideBodyStyle: { marginTop: '10px', fontSize: '12.5px', lineHeight: 1.6, color: 'var(--ink70)', textWrap: 'pretty' },
      // The journal's filter row scrolls too on a phone, same reason as the
      // tour and news rows (2026-09-07): four chips of uneven width wrap into a
      // ragged second line otherwise.
      jrnFilterRowStyle: narrow
        ? { marginTop: '20px', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '8px', flexWrap: 'nowrap',
            overflowX: 'auto', overflowY: 'hidden', scrollbarWidth: 'none',
            WebkitOverflowScrolling: 'touch', paddingBottom: '2px',
            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.12em', textTransform: 'uppercase' }
        : {
        marginTop: '20px', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '8px', flexWrap: 'wrap',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.12em', textTransform: 'uppercase'
      },
      jrnFilters: ['All', 'Interview', 'Review', 'List'].map(f => {
        const on = (st.jrnFilter || 'All') === f;
        return {
          key: 'jf' + f, label: f,
          box: {
            display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', height: narrow ? '44px' : '32px', padding: '0 13px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
            borderRadius: '5px', cursor: 'pointer', whiteSpace: 'nowrap', flexShrink: 0,
            border: '1px solid ' + (on ? 'rgba(138,106,18,0.6)' : 'var(--hair20)'),
            background: on ? 'rgba(138,106,18,0.1)' : 'transparent',
            color: on ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink70)', fontWeight: on ? 700 : 400
          },
          click: () => this.setState({ jrnFilter: f })
        };
      }),
      jrnCount: jrnList.length + (jrnList.length === 1 ? ' piece' : ' pieces'),
      jrnCountStyle: { color: 'var(--ink40)', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' },
      jrnHasLead: jrnList.length > 0,
      jrnLeadWrapStyle: narrow
        ? { marginTop: '22px', display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: '14px' }
        : { marginTop: '26px', display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: '1fr 1fr', gap: '30px', alignItems: 'start' },
      // contain, not cover (2026-09-05) — see the article hero's note. The
      // journal's lead is the same portrait the article page shows, so it was
      // cropping the same face in a second place.
      // The lead's destination, same root-relative form as the cards (§3).
      // §1a · THE LONG PATH (2026-09-07). r39 shipped these at '/journal/<slug>'
      // and you measured that it does not resolve; the anchors already in
      // readers' hands point at nothing. addr('journal') is the long form, and
      // it is the only place the journal address is written.
      // ⚠ ATH's domain question (.music vs .space) is still with Tom and does
      // NOT affect this: the path is relative, so it resolves on whichever host
      // serves ATH.
      jrnLeadSlug: jrnLead ? jrnLead[0] : '',
      jrnLeadHref: this.addr('journal', jrnLead ? jrnLead[0] : ''),
      jrnLeadHeroStyle: {
        width: '100%', height: narrow ? '200px' : '280px', borderRadius: '7px',
        border: '1px solid var(--hair)', cursor: 'pointer',
        // cover at 30%, matching the deck's featured lead and the cards
        // (2026-09-06): every journal LISTING image crops and fills. Only the
        // article page shows a whole frame.
        background: 'url(' + this.res(jrnLead ? jrnLead[5] : '') + ') center 30%/cover no-repeat, var(--tile)'
      },
      jrnMetaRowStyle: {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '12px', flexWrap: 'wrap',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase'
      },
      jrnLeadKicker: jrnLead ? jrnLead[1] : '',
      jrnLeadWhere: jrnLead ? jrnLead[6] : '',
      jrnLeadTitle: jrnLead ? jrnLead[2] : '',
      jrnLeadTitleStyle: { marginTop: '10px', fontSize: narrow ? '20px' : '26px', fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: '-0.02em', lineHeight: 1.15, color: 'var(--ink)', textWrap: 'pretty' },
      jrnLeadDek: jrnLead ? jrnLead[3] : '',
      jrnLeadDekStyle: { marginTop: '10px', fontSize: '13.5px', lineHeight: 1.6, maxWidth: '58ch', color: 'var(--ink70)', textWrap: 'pretty' },
      jrnLeadByline: jrnLead ? jrnLead[4] : '',
      jrnBylineStyle: { marginTop: '10px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.1em', color: 'var(--ink55)', textWrap: 'pretty' },
      jrnLeadRead: jrnLead ? ('Read the ' + String(jrnLead[1]).toLowerCase() + ' →') : '',
      jrnReadStyle: {
        textDecoration: 'none',
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', width: 'max-content', marginTop: '14px',
        height: '36px', padding: '0 14px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '5px',
        border: '1px solid rgba(138,106,18,0.6)', cursor: 'pointer',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.12em',
        textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--gold)'
      },
      jrnRunHeadStyle: {
        marginTop: '32px', display: jrnRest.length ? 'flex' : 'none', alignItems: 'center', gap: '14px',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '10px', letterSpacing: '0.2em',
        textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)'
      },
      jrnRunLabel: 'More from the zine',
      jrnGridStyle: {
        marginTop: '16px', display: 'grid',
        gridTemplateColumns: 'repeat(' + (narrow ? 1 : Math.min(3, Math.max(1, jrnRest.length))) + ', minmax(0, 1fr))',
        gap: '26px 22px'
      },
      jrnRest: jrnRest.map(x => ({
        key: 'jr' + x[0],
        // §4-7 · THE HANDLE THE ARTICLE BODY ALREADY HAS (2026-09-07). The body
        // slot carries data-article-slug and engineering said that is the right
        // shape; the CARDS in the list had nothing, so the only handle on them
        // was the article TITLE — the one corpus guaranteed to collide. Same
        // slug, same attribute name, so a card and the page it opens agree.
        slug: x[0],
        card: { display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', minWidth: 0, cursor: 'pointer', textDecoration: 'none', color: 'inherit' },
        // COVER on the listing (2026-09-06, Tom): "stretch and crop the photos
      // for the page itself". A card is a tile in a grid, so a uniform crop is
      // what makes the grid read as one — the article page shows the whole frame.
      hero: { width: '100%', height: narrow ? '180px' : '160px', borderRadius: '7px', border: '1px solid var(--hair)', background: 'url(' + this.res(x[5]) + ') center 30%/cover no-repeat, var(--tile)' },
        metaRow: { marginTop: '12px', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '10px', flexWrap: 'wrap', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase' },
        kicker: x[1], where: x[6], title: x[2], dek: x[3], byline: x[4],
        // ── §3 · A DESTINATION, NOT A STATE FLIP (2026-09-05) ─────────────
        // The handler fires and changes state, so the card felt dead: nothing
        // navigates and there is no address to route. Every journal card is an
        // anchor to a ROOT-RELATIVE path now — /journal/<slug> — which is the
        // one form that answers the ask without my guessing a domain (the
        // .music vs .space question in PACKAGE-REPLY is still open, and a
        // relative path is correct under either). The click still runs so the
        // in-file preview flips, and the host can intercept for a client-side
        // transition; the href is what a refresh, a copy and a crawler use.
        // This is DQ-61's rule applied to a card: if a visitor can click it,
        // it has a real URL.
        href: this.addr('journal', x[0]),
        titleStyle: { marginTop: '8px', fontSize: '16px', fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: '-0.015em', lineHeight: 1.2, color: 'var(--ink)', textWrap: 'pretty' },
        dekStyle: { marginTop: '7px', fontSize: '12.5px', lineHeight: 1.55, color: 'var(--ink70)', textWrap: 'pretty' },
        bylineStyle: { marginTop: '8px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.1em', color: 'var(--ink55)', textWrap: 'pretty' },
        click: this.navTo(() => this.setState({ route: 'ath-article', article: this.data('JOURNAL').indexOf(x), browseOpen: false, explore: false }))
      })),
      jrnFootStyle: {
        marginTop: '32px', paddingTop: '18px', borderTop: '1px solid var(--hair)',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.14em',
        textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)', fontStyle: 'italic'
      },
      jrnFoot: 'Volume Two’s pieces are commissioned — pending',
      // ── THE BODY BLOCKS (2026-09-05) ──────────────────────────────────────
      // One treatment per kind, keyed off the piece's own slug. An interview's
      // `speech` is the kind that earns its own dress — a named speaker set
      // above the answer, so a conversation reads as one; `entry` is the
      // photographed list row (name · place, then the blurb), which is the
      // shape the top-fives and the artist round-ups use.
      // No blocks → the section renders nothing at all, per § 23. The specimen
      // shell is gone: grey bars are a picture of an article, not an article.
      athBody: (() => {
        const slug = (() => {
          const list = this.data('JOURNAL_LIST');
          const x = list.length ? list[Math.min(list.length - 1, Math.max(0, st.article || 0))] : null;
          return x ? x[6] : '';
        })();
        const blocks = (this.data('ARTICLE_BODY') || {})[slug] || [];
        return blocks.map((b, i) => {
          const kind = String(b[0] || 'p');
          const base = { key: 'ab' + i, kind: kind };
          const hide = { display: 'none' };
          const row = Object.assign({}, base, {
            text: '', speaker: '', name: '', place: '', blurb: '',
            wrapStyle: { display: 'block' },
            textStyle: hide, speakerStyle: hide, asideStyle: hide,
            entryStyle: hide, entryPhotoStyle: hide, entryNameStyle: hide, entryBlurbStyle: hide,
            noteStyle: hide, signoffStyle: hide
          });
          if (kind === 'speech') {
            row.speaker = b[1] || '';
            row.text = b[2] || '';
            row.speakerStyle = { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8.5px', fontWeight: 700, letterSpacing: '0.2em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--gold)' };
            row.textStyle = { marginTop: '6px', fontSize: '15.5px', lineHeight: 1.75, color: 'var(--ink)', textWrap: 'pretty' };
          } else if (kind === 'aside') {
            row.text = b[1] || '';
            row.asideStyle = { padding: '2px 0 2px 18px', borderLeft: '2px solid var(--hair20)', fontSize: '14px', lineHeight: 1.7, color: 'var(--ink55)', fontStyle: 'italic', textWrap: 'pretty' };
          } else if (kind === 'entry') {
            row.name = (b[1] || '') + (b[2] ? '  ·  ' + b[2] : '');
            row.blurb = b[3] || '';
            row.wrapStyle = { display: 'flex', gap: '14px', alignItems: 'flex-start' };
            row.entryPhotoStyle = b[4]
              ? { width: '84px', height: '84px', flexShrink: 0, borderRadius: '5px', background: 'url(' + this.res(b[4]) + ') center/cover no-repeat, var(--tile)' }
              : { width: '84px', height: '84px', flexShrink: 0, borderRadius: '5px', background: 'repeating-linear-gradient(135deg, var(--tile) 0 6px, transparent 6px 12px)' };
            row.entryNameStyle = { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)' };
            row.entryBlurbStyle = { marginTop: '6px', fontSize: '14.5px', lineHeight: 1.7, color: 'var(--ink)', textWrap: 'pretty' };
          } else if (kind === 'note') {
            row.text = b[1] || '';
            row.noteStyle = { marginTop: '6px', paddingTop: '16px', borderTop: '1px solid var(--hair)', fontSize: '14px', lineHeight: 1.7, color: 'var(--ink70)', textWrap: 'pretty' };
          } else if (kind === 'signoff') {
            row.text = b[1] || '';
            row.signoffStyle = { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink40)' };
          } else {
            row.text = b[1] || '';
            row.textStyle = { fontSize: '15.5px', lineHeight: 1.75, color: 'var(--ink)', textWrap: 'pretty' };
          }
          return row;
        });
      })(),
      // ── THE BODY REGION IS ALWAYS THERE (2026-09-05, Tom) ─────────────────
      // Tom: "I don't want to read the published article. I want the text… They
      // just don't have a proper space to put it." Right on both counts, and
      // the second is the actionable one. r25 hid the region when ARTICLE_BODY
      // was empty and left an outbound link in its place — so the page offered
      // a way to read the piece SOMEWHERE ELSE, which is not what an article
      // page is for, and a build looking for where the text goes found nothing
      // on screen to aim at.
      // The region now renders unconditionally, is marked as a mount slot like
      // data-star-field and data-video-slot, and carries the slug it is keyed
      // by. Empty, it states the table, the key and the six kinds — an
      // instruction to the build, in the place the text will occupy.
      athBodyStyle: { marginTop: '26px', maxWidth: '68ch', display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: '18px' },
      athSlug: (() => {
        const list = this.data('JOURNAL_LIST');
        const x = list.length ? list[Math.min(list.length - 1, Math.max(0, st.article || 0))] : null;
        return x ? (x[6] || '') : '';
      })(),
      athBodyPending: (() => {
        const list = this.data('JOURNAL_LIST');
        const x = list.length ? list[Math.min(list.length - 1, Math.max(0, st.article || 0))] : null;
        const slug = x ? (x[6] || '') : '';
        const blocks = (this.data('ARTICLE_BODY') || {})[slug] || [];
        if (blocks.length) return '';
        return 'The body of this piece renders here, from ARTICLE_BODY[\u2018' + (slug || 'slug') + '\u2019] \u2014 one block per paragraph, quoted answer, aside, list entry, note and signoff. Nothing else on this page stands in for it.';
      })(),
      athBodyPendingStyle: (() => {
        const list = this.data('JOURNAL_LIST');
        const x = list.length ? list[Math.min(list.length - 1, Math.max(0, st.article || 0))] : null;
        const blocks = (this.data('ARTICLE_BODY') || {})[x ? x[6] : ''] || [];
        return blocks.length
          ? { display: 'none' }
          : { padding: '18px 0', borderTop: '1px solid var(--hair)', borderBottom: '1px solid var(--hair)', fontSize: '13px', lineHeight: 1.7, color: 'var(--ink55)', fontStyle: 'italic', textWrap: 'pretty' };
      })(),
      // The dress the deleted control wore, unchanged apart from the top
      // margin, which now spaces it from the body rather than from a grey bar.
      // ── DEMOTED (2026-09-05, Tom) ─────────────────────────────────────────
      // As a gold bordered button below an empty region it read as the page's
      // ANSWER — "the text is elsewhere" — which is the opposite of what an
      // article page says. It is a quiet trailing line now: still the route to

      // ⚠ THE DEMOTION OVERSHOT (2026-09-05, second pass). Demoting it to

      // NON-interactive meta lines, and 11px contradicts the touch-targets

      //
      // What the demotion actually needed was losing the BUTTON — the gold

        letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
        color: 'var(--gold)', cursor: 'pointer', textDecoration: 'none',
        borderBottom: 'none'
      },
      // The rule sits on the text, not on the 44px box, so the underline is

      athReadInkStyle: { borderBottom: '1px solid var(--goldsoft)', paddingBottom: '3px' },
      athCurrent: (() => {
        const list = this.data('JOURNAL_LIST');
        // No articles supplied (site, before the journal is wired) → an empty

        const x = list.length ? list[Math.min(list.length - 1, Math.max(0, st.article || 0))] : ['', '', '', '', '', '', ''];
        return {
          kicker: x[0], title: x[1], dek: x[2], byline: x[3], where: x[5],
          // ── THE WHOLE PHOTOGRAPH, NEVER A CROP THROUGH A FACE (2026-09-05,
        // Tom) ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
        // `height: 300px` with `cover` forced every hero into one letterbox
        // and cut whatever did not fit — Stelth Ulvang's head off above the
        // mouth, Yuka Honda's face bisected. These are portraits; a fixed band
        // is the wrong container for them.
        //
        // `contain` shows the whole frame, and the box takes the photograph's
        // OWN ratio when the site sends it as JOURNAL_LIST[7] — then there is
        // no letterboxing either, because the box is the picture's shape. With
        // no ratio supplied it falls back to 16/9 and `contain` still keeps
        // every face intact, which is the part that must not depend on data
        // arriving. The ground is --tile so any margin reads as a mount rather
        // than as a gap.
        // ── THE HERO CROPS; THE CARDS DO NOT (2026-09-06, Tom) ─────────────
        // r27 made every journal photograph `contain` because the article hero
        // was cutting through faces. Right for the cards and the rail — a
        // thumbnail of a portrait should show the portrait — but wrong for the
        // MASTHEAD image, which is a masking box: `contain` letterboxed it into
        // a strip floating in grey, which is what the screenshot shows.
        // A hero is a crop by nature; it just must not be a 300px letterbox
        // cropping through a face. So: `cover` again, with the photograph's own
        // ratio (JOURNAL_LIST[7]) as the box, so the crop is the frame's edges
        // rather than a band through the middle — and 16/9 as the fallback,
        // which is a shape a press photo survives.
        // ── THE ARTICLE SHOWS THE WHOLE PHOTOGRAPH (2026-09-06, Tom) ───────

        //
        // The rule, stated where it matters: a THUMBNAIL crops (it is a tile,
        // and a letterboxed tile breaks the grid it sits in — that is why the
        // 54px rail thumbs beside this are cover); the ARTICLE'S OWN IMAGE does
        // not. Here the picture is the piece, at the photograph's own ratio, so

        // This declared `x[7] || 16/9` and drew the photograph with contain over
        // var(--tile) — so a guessed ratio, a photo fitted inside it, and the
        // tile painting the difference: the box Tom asked to have removed. The
        // fix went into V1 and V2 kept the box.
        //
        // Stated beats measured beats guessed: x[7] when the data supplies it,
        // imgAR off the file itself when it does not. No tile, no border, no
        // radius, left top — so there is nothing left to letterbox against.
        heroStyle: (() => {
          const src = this.res(x[4]);
          const ar = x[7] || this.imgAR(src) || 1;
          return {
            width: '100%', maxWidth: narrow ? '100%' : '620px',
            aspectRatio: String(this.arOf(ar)),
            background: 'url(' + src + ') left top/contain no-repeat'

        })(),
          url: 'https://acrossthehorizon.space/journal/' + x[6] + '.html'
        };
      })(),
      athPerks: [

      // ── THE VOLUME BANNER IS DERIVED (2026-09-04, designer request 7) ────

      athBannerShape: (() => {
        const vols = this.data('VOLS') || [];
        const v = vols[0];
        if (!v || !v.drops || !v.drops.length) return '';
        const per = v.drops.length;
        const people = 1 + ((v.drops[0][3] || []).length);

        return word(per).replace(/^./, c => c.toUpperCase()) + ' drops a volume · ' + word(people) + ' artists a drop';

      athBannerState: (() => {
        const v = this.newestReleased();
        const vols = this.data('VOLS') || [];
        const cur = (v && v.vol) ? vols[v.vol - 1] : vols[vols.length - 1];
        const drops = (cur && cur.drops) || [];
        if (!drops.length) return '';
        const out = drops.filter(d => d[4] !== 0).map(d => d[0]);
        const held = drops.filter(d => d[4] === 0).map(d => d[0]);
        const run = (a) => a.length > 2 && a[a.length - 1] - a[0] === a.length - 1 ? (a[0] + '–' + a[a.length - 1]) : a.join(', ');
        if (!held.length) return 'Drops ' + run(out) + ' out';
        if (!out.length) return 'Drops ' + run(held) + ' finished, unreleased';
        return 'Drops ' + run(out) + ' out · ' + run(held) + ' finished, unreleased';
      })(),

      // ── THE PLAY CONTROLS EMIT (2026-09-04, ledger 16) ────────────────────
      // "The site owns audio; this file owns every play control" — but these

      // index into the DISPLAYED list for that kind, zero-based** — the list
      // after the viewport cap (5 desktop, 2 phone, 1 short phone) and after

      // this file capped and drew from. row 0 on the gold control means "the

      playClick: (e) => {
        if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
        // ⚠ IT EMITTED WITHOUT SETTING PLAY STATE, which looked identical from
        // the outside: a press that changed nothing on the page. The gold button

        // pressing the button turns row 01's ▶︎ to ❙❙, because they are one thing.
        const feed = (this.data('BROADCAST') || []).filter(b => String(b[0]).toLowerCase() === String(s[0]).toLowerCase());

        this.playExt('bc-' + String(s[0]).toLowerCase() + '-0', s[2], first ? String(first[1] || '') : String(s[2] || ''));

      },
      linkClick: (e) => {

        titleStyle: { marginTop: aTight ? '7px' : '10px', fontSize: aTight ? '16px' : aNarrow ? '18px' : '24px', fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: '-0.02em', lineHeight: 1.15, color: 'var(--ink)' },

      // At 1728 · 16" both fit unscaled. So five is not "one screen without

      //
      // Phone stays two and a SHORT phone stays one (ruling 8) — those cuts are

      // ── THE ROWS ARE THE FEED'S (2026-09-04, designer request 10) ────────
      // Every row repeated ATH_STRANDS[6] — one label, N times, with no date.

      // The third surface where a dead feed must not read as "no episodes

      // 'empty' or absent falls through to the strand's own pending note,
      // which already explains why a strand has nothing yet.
      stateNote: (() => {
        const state = this.data('BROADCAST_STATE');
        if (state !== 'failed') return '';
        return 'The feed did not load. Try again shortly.';
      })(),
      stateNoteStyle: (this.data('BROADCAST_STATE') === 'failed')
        ? { marginTop: '10px', fontSize: '12px', lineHeight: 1.5, color: 'var(--ink55)', fontStyle: 'italic' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      rows: (() => {
        // aTight / aNarrow, like the rest of this builder (2026-09-09): the
        // strand's GEOMETRY was ported to the column's width and its row cap was

        const cap = aTight ? 1 : aNarrow ? 2 : 5;

        if (this.data('BROADCAST_STATE') === 'failed') return [];
        const feed = (this.data('BROADCAST') || []).filter(b => String(b[0]).toLowerCase() === String(s[0]).toLowerCase());

          return feed.slice(0, cap).map((b, n) => ({

            // r53 removed the literal "—:—" (Tom: "still no track times or
            // episode lengths. It's just an empty timestamp") and made the cell
            // conditional on BROADCAST[5], "the duration when the feed carries
            // one".
            //
            // ⚠ BROADCAST[5] IS THE EPISODE URL, NOT A DURATION. This file has
            // always read it that way — linkClick does `(feed[0] || [])[5]` and
            // hands it to window.open. So on a real feed the cell printed the
            // link: a full flowstate.fm / nts.live URL in a 10px mono column,
            // which then took the row's width and collapsed the title to about
            // ninety pixels, one word per line. Visible in the 2026-09-07
            // screenshot of the live broadcast page.
            //
            // I guessed an index instead of reading the one place that already
            // used it, which is the exact failure this file's "one authority"
            // rule exists to prevent — and the guess only surfaced once real
            // data arrived, because the mock feed is empty.
            //
            // THERE IS NO DURATION FIELD IN BROADCAST, and the ask for one is
            // WITHDRAWN (2026-09-09, code audit part 1 and 8b). Two reasons, and
            // either is enough. (a) The index named was BROADCAST[6], which is
            // already the episode AUDIO URL — declared at the table and read by
            // the play controls — so one slot was being asked of engineering for
            // two facts at once. (b) The feed has no runtime to send: RSS
            // `enclosure length` is a byte count and <itunes:duration> is absent
            // from every item on the live feed, so the column would be empty
            // always — not an unknown value, no data source. If a feed ever
            // carries one, name a FREE index and this cell comes back.
            // Until they do, the row is number, glyph, title · date, ⋮, and it
            // says nothing it does not know.
            // A row is a play control too (ledger 16) — it was inert.
            // A row is a play control too (ledger 16) — it was inert, and then
            // it emitted without setting play state, which looked identical from
            // the outside: a press that changed nothing on the page.
            // V2 · in HOVER mode the row's ▶︎ goes and only ❙❙ — state — stays,
            // the same rule the album tracklist and the ATH drop rows follow.
            // (2026-09-09 audit: the last literal ▶︎ in the file, so Broadcast
            // was showing play glyphs in a mode that has none.) The CELL stays
            // either way: a fixed 16px flex item, so an empty string costs no
            // layout and nothing shifts between modes.
            glyph: this.extOn('bc-' + String(s[0]).toLowerCase() + '-' + n) ? '❙❙' : (this.playMode(st) ? '▶︎' : ''),
            glyphStyle: {
              width: '16px', flexShrink: 0, fontSize: '10px', cursor: 'pointer',
              color: this.extOn('bc-' + String(s[0]).toLowerCase() + '-' + n) ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink55)'
            },
            // The whole row lights while it is the one sounding, the same way an
            // album tracklist row does — a 10px glyph is not enough to find at a
            // glance, and Tom's ask was that it be very clear what is playing.
            rowStyle: {
              display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '12px',
              padding: '0 8px 0 0', borderRadius: '4px',
              background: this.extOn('bc-' + String(s[0]).toLowerCase() + '-' + n) ? 'rgba(138,106,18,0.14)' : 'transparent',

            labelStyle: {
              flex: 1, fontSize: '13px', fontStyle: 'italic', cursor: 'pointer',
              color: this.extOn('bc-' + String(s[0]).toLowerCase() + '-' + n) ? 'var(--ink)' : 'var(--ink55)'
            },
            click: (e) => {
              if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();

            // ever have spoken about a show rather than an episode. The
            // identity travels in ctxEpisode — the strand's kind and this

              borderRadius: '4px', cursor: 'pointer', fontSize: '14px', color: 'var(--ink40)'
            },

              if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
              this.setState({ ctxEpisode: { kind: String(s[0]).toLowerCase(), row: n } });
              return this.openCtx('episode', null)(e);
            }
          }));
        }
        return [];
      })()

      // ATH's ☰ opens ATH's OWN nav, not the label list. navItems branched on
