      // offering Releases / Tour / Radio — the four-label nav on a site that has
      // none of those. Same list navItems uses, same handlers.
      // ── THE GROUP AND CLANDESTINE GET THE SAME DRAWER (2026-09-05, Tom) ───
      // "There's no explore menu in the header on mobile, so I can't get back
      // to the label sites easily", and Clandestine's four header controls
      // needed collapsing too. Both headers were built for desktop widths: on
      // the group, Explore measured 0px wide — squeezed out of the flex row
      // rather than hidden, so nothing said it was missing; on Clandestine the
      // four controls ran 400px past the stage edge.
      // Neither needed a new pattern. The label header already has a hamburger
      // and a sheet, and the sheet is top-level markup, so it renders on every
      // route. These two surfaces just needed to be IN the list and to carry
      // the button — and "Explore the group" is already a row in that sheet,
      // which is exactly the way back to the label sites.
      menuItems: (clsPage != null
        // Clandestine: its own destinations. The SERVICES are a dropdown, not
        // drawer rows (2026-09-05, Tom) — "Our services" opens the same panel
        // the desktop button opens, as a sheet, so the drawer stays three short
        // rows instead of nine.
        ? [['Client portal', 'x:portal'], ['See our work', 'x:work'], ['Our services', 'x:services']]
        : route === 'group'
        // The group's four in-page sections, which navStyle hides on narrow.
        ? [['Label group', 'x:#labels'], ['Services', 'x:#services'], ['Software', 'x:#software'], ['Contact', 'x:#contact']]
        : athSite
        // VOLUMES IS A ROUTE, NOT A CARD (2026-09-02, Tom). It used to be
        // ['Volumes', 3] — deck card 3, the latest-drops panel. It now goes to
        // the full releases page, 'ath-all-releases' ("Every drop, newest
        // first.", the 16 DROPS · TWO VOLUMES count line). The drops CARD keeps
        // its own 'See all releases →' to the same place, so the card is a
        // preview and the nav item is the catalogue — which is what a nav item
        // called Volumes should mean.
        // The click handler already branches on the type: a number is goCard,
        // a string is goRoute. No other change needed.
        // ARTISTS SITS WITH THE CATALOGUE, NOT AFTER THE PROGRAMME (2026-09-04).
        // Tom asked for Artists to be "added" — it was already here, but LAST,
        // after Podcast and Journal, which is why it read as missing: the eye
        // stops at the programme items and never reaches it. The order is now
        // the site's three halves in turn — what the series IS (the idea, the
        // map), what it HAS (Volumes, Artists), and what it PUBLISHES (Podcast,
        // Journal). Both the desktop nav and the phone drawer read this array.
        ? [['The idea', 0], ['Curator Map', 2], ['Volumes', 'ath-all-releases'], ['Artists', 'ath-artists'], ['Podcast', 4], ['Journal', 5]]
        : LABEL_MENU
      ).map((m, mi) => {
        const on = athSite
          ? (typeof m[1] === 'number' ? (route === 'ath-map' && (st.panel || 0) === m[1]) : route === m[1])
          : (st.view === m[1] || (st.view === 'album' && st.prev === m[1]));
        return {
          key: m[1], label: m[0],
          // ── THE PHONE NAV, DESIGNED (2026-09-06, Tom) ─────────────────────
          // It was 52px rows of 15px Archivo with hairline rules — which is
          // what every mobile site's menu looks like, because it is what you
          // get when nobody decides. Four changes, each borrowed from this
          // file's own vocabulary rather than invented:
          //
          //  1 · A NUMBER. 01, 02, 03 in mono, gold on the page you are on.
          //      The hero rail, the tracklists and the drop rows all number
          //      this way, so the nav now belongs to the same family instead
          //      of being the one list in the file without a rhythm.
          //  2 · DISPLAY TYPE, not list type. 22px at -0.02em, weight 600 —
          //      the treatment the artist name and record title use. A
          //      destination should read like the page it opens; 15px regular
          //      is what made it read like a settings row.
          //  3 · THE RIGHT SIDE CARRIES A COUNT (menuCount). That is what the
          //      dead space was for: this file derives every count already, so
          //      the nav can say how much is behind each door — 489 records,
          //      306 artists, 16 drops. Absent where there is no honest
          //      number, per § 23.
          //  4 · A GOLD EDGE marks the current row, instead of colour alone.
          //
          // The rows also arrive in sequence (sa-nav-in, 26ms apart) so the
          // sheet reads as composed rather than dumped.
          num: String(mi + 1).padStart(2, '0'),
          // ── §4 of r47 · THE PHONE DRAWER GETS THE SAME ADDRESSES ───────────
          // You measured six real anchors at 375×812 with correct addresses and
          // all six `visible: false` — they were the DESKTOP menu, hidden at
          // phone width — and the drawer that replaced them had no anchors at
          // all. So on a phone none of the site navigation was copyable.
          //
          // Same addr() authority as the desktop row, so the two can never name
          // different addresses. 'x:' rows are this surface's own actions (an
          // anchor jump, the portal, the work band) has no address, so it gets
          // no href.
          //
          // ⚠ ATH'S DECK CARDS DO HAVE ONE NOW (2026-09-11, Tom) — they were
          // listed here as addressless alongside the anchor jumps and they are
          // not the same thing. An anchor jump has nowhere else to be; a card
          // is a page that had simply never been given a path.
          href: (() => {
            const k = m[1];
            if (typeof k === 'number') return isAth ? this.addr('ath-card', k) : null;
            const s = String(k);
            if (s.indexOf('x:') === 0) return null;
            if (s.indexOf('r:') === 0) return this.addr(s.slice(2));
            if (s === 'ath-all-releases') return this.addr('releases');
            if (s === 'ath-artists') return this.addr('artists');
            return this.addr(s);
          })(),
          numStyle: narrow
            ? { width: '26px', flexShrink: 0, fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.1em', color: on ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink40)', fontVariantNumeric: 'tabular-nums' }
            : { display: 'none' },
          count: narrow ? this.menuCount(m[1]) : '',
          countStyle: narrow
            ? { flexShrink: 0, fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: on ? 'rgba(138,106,18,0.75)' : 'var(--ink40)', fontVariantNumeric: 'tabular-nums' }
            : { display: 'none' },
          labelStyle: narrow
            ? { flex: 1, minWidth: 0, fontSize: '17px', fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: '-0.015em', lineHeight: 1.1, color: on ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink)' }
            : { flex: 1, minWidth: 0 },
          box: narrow
            ? { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '14px', minHeight: '46px', flexShrink: 0, boxSizing: 'border-box',
                paddingLeft: '12px', marginLeft: '-12px',
                borderLeft: '2px solid ' + (on ? 'var(--gold)' : 'transparent'),
                borderBottom: '1px solid var(--hair)', cursor: 'pointer',
                animation: 'sa-nav-in 260ms cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1) both',
                animationDelay: (mi * 26) + 'ms' }
            : {
            display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', height: '40px', padding: '0 12px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
            borderRadius: '5px', cursor: 'pointer',
            background: on ? 'var(--tile)' : 'transparent',
            color: on ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink70)',
            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '10px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase'
          },
          click: this.navTo(() => {
            this.setState({ menuOpen: false });
            // 'x:' rows are this surface's own actions rather than routes
            // (2026-09-05): an anchor jump, the portal, the work band.
            if (String(m[1]).indexOf('x:') === 0) {
              const what = String(m[1]).slice(2);
              if (what === 'portal') { if (typeof window !== 'undefined') window.open('https://releasemanager.net/#/portal/login', '_blank', 'noopener'); return; }
              if (what === 'work') { setTimeout(() => this.clsSeeWorkNow(), 30); return; }
              // The services panel as a sheet — the same panel the desktop
              // button opens (2026-09-05).
              if (what === 'services') { this.setState({ clsMenu: true }); return; }
              if (typeof window !== 'undefined') window.location.hash = what.replace('#', '');
              return;
            }
            if (String(m[1]).indexOf('r:') === 0) return this.goRoute(String(m[1]).slice(2));
            // ⚠ athSite, NOT isAth (2026-09-09, Tom: "on the ATH star map I
            // can't get any of the sidebar contents to come up"). isAth is the
            // ROUTE prefix — route.indexOf('ath-') === 0 — and the ATH star map
            // is route 'star', so it was FALSE there and this branch never ran.
            // ATH's items then fell through to my isStar branch below and were
            // handed to go(), which set st.view to a deck-card NUMBER (0, 2, 4,
            // 5) or an ath-* route string. No drawer view matches any of those,
            // so the column rendered nothing at all.
            //
            // §1 is explicit about which test to use: "identity travels with the
            // visitor, layout belongs to the route", and this is an identity
            // question — whose menu is this. athSite is labelKeyOf's answer and
            // is true on 'star' and 'catalogue' reached from ATH, which is
            // exactly the case that broke.
            if (athSite) {
              // ── TWO OF ATH'S SIX HAVE A COLUMN FORM (2026-09-09, Tom) ──────
              // "I'm having trouble getting any of the sidebar contents to come
              // up." Right, and fixing the identity test last round only changed
              // HOW it failed: before, the view was set to a deck-card number
              // and the column went blank; after, the route changed and the
              // column went away. Both leave him with no sidebar.
              //
              // ATH's menu names deck CARDS (0, 2, 4, 5) and full ROUTES
              // (ath-all-releases, ath-artists) — not drawer view keys — so
              // there was no mapping from an item to a column at all. Two of the
              // six have exact equivalents, and the column already renders ATH's
              // own rows under ATH identity (measured: "Volumes · Across the
              // Horizon · 9 of 179", "Artists · Across the Horizon · 8 with
              // records"), so this is a mapping rather than new content.
              //
              // ── ONE DECK, SEVEN CARDS, AND THE MENU ANCHORS INTO IT ─────────
              // (2026-09-09, Tom: "Each card is one of the pages from the
              // hamburger menu.") A menu item's number IS its card index in the
              // landing deck — the same index the grid deck's goCard() takes —
              // so the column needs no second mapping: open the deck, scroll to
              // that card. The three interim column views this replaces
              // (ath-idea, ath-podcast, ath-journal) are deleted: they were
              // narrower retellings of cards the column now carries whole.
              // Volumes IS the column's last card — every drop, newest first —
              // rather than the flat releases list (2026-09-09, Tom).
              if (isStar && m[1] === 'ath-all-releases') { this.holdHover(2000); return this.athColOpen(7); }
              if (isStar && m[1] === 'ath-artists') { this.holdHover(2000); return this.go('artists', true); }
              if (isStar && typeof m[1] === 'number') { this.holdHover(2000); return this.athColOpen(m[1]); }
              return typeof m[1] === 'number' ? this.goCard(m[1]) : this.goRoute(m[1]);
            }
            // ── ON THE MAP, THE HAMBURGER FILLS THE COLUMN (2026-09-09, Tom) ─
            // ⚠ I WIRED THE DESKTOP NAV AND NOT THIS ONE. The hamburger has its
            // own list — menuItems, built from the same LABEL_MENU but with its
            // own click handler — so changing navItems' handler did nothing for
            // the control Tom actually uses. He reported it twice and was right
            // both times; the second report is what found the second list.
            //
            // The comment this replaces said "same rule as the desktop nav",
            // which was true when written and became false the moment the
            // desktop nav's rule changed. A comment asserting agreement with
            // another mechanism is exactly the kind that rots silently — so the
            // two now share one expression rather than two copies of one rule.
            // ── TWO SECONDS TO REACH THE COLUMN (2026-09-09, Tom) ─────────
            // Choosing an item on the map fills the sidebar and the visitor then
            // has to cross the whole star field to get to it. Every star on that
            // path armed a preview and every panel they clipped opened. So all
            // hover states hold for two seconds after the choice — see
            // holdHover for why this one cannot cancel on movement the way the
            // view-switch mute does.
            //
            // ONLY WHEN THE COLUMN IS THE DESTINATION. Off the map the item
            // opens a full page and there is no trip to protect; opening the
            // menu and choosing nothing arms nothing, because this sits on the
            // item's click and not on the hamburger's.
            // The four labels only: their menu names drawer VIEWS ('artists',
            // 'tour', 'radio'…) and ATH's names deck cards and its own routes,
            // handled above. Reached only when athSite is false, so the athSite
            // return is what keeps them apart.
            if (isStar) { this.holdHover(2000); return this.go(m[1]); }
            this.goRoute(m[1] === 'releases' ? 'catalogue' : m[1]);
          })
        };
      }),
      // One nav, driven by data. The four-label template carries Releases /
      // Artists / Tour / News / Follow Us / About; Across the Horizon carries
      // its own — Journal and Broadcast in place of Tour and News, and Subscribe
      // as a pill (15b). Same block, same hover behaviour either way.
      // ── §1a · THE NAV IS ANCHORS (2026-09-07) ─────────────────────────
      // Six items × five labels on every page, and they were spans. addr() gives
      // each its real path; ATH's deck cards get none, because a card in a deck
      // has no address — an <a> with no href is not a link and behaves exactly
      // as the span did, which is the honest rendering of "no destination".
      navItems: athSite
        // VOLUMES IS A ROUTE, NOT A CARD (2026-09-02, Tom). It used to be
        // ['Volumes', 3] — deck card 3, the latest-drops panel. It now goes to
        // the full releases page, 'ath-all-releases' ("Every drop, newest
        // first.", the 16 DROPS · TWO VOLUMES count line). The drops CARD keeps
        // its own 'See all releases →' to the same place, so the card is a
        // preview and the nav item is the catalogue — which is what a nav item
        // called Volumes should mean.
        // The click handler already branches on the type: a number is goCard,
        // a string is goRoute. No other change needed.
        // ARTISTS SITS WITH THE CATALOGUE, NOT AFTER THE PROGRAMME (2026-09-04).
        // Tom asked for Artists to be "added" — it was already here, but LAST,
        // after Podcast and Journal, which is why it read as missing: the eye
        // stops at the programme items and never reaches it. The order is now
        // the site's three halves in turn — what the series IS (the idea, the
        // map), what it HAS (Volumes, Artists), and what it PUBLISHES (Podcast,
        // Journal). Both the desktop nav and the phone drawer read this array.
        ? [['The idea', 0], ['Curator Map', 2], ['Volumes', 'ath-all-releases'], ['Artists', 'ath-artists'], ['Podcast', 4], ['Journal', 5]]
            .map(n => ({
              key: 'nv' + n[1], label: n[0],
              // A deck card has no address; a route does. ath-all-releases and
              // ath-artists are ATH's own views, so they take the label-view
              // form — /releases and /artists on ATH's own domain.
              // Deck cards address as their slug (2026-09-11, Tom): /the-idea,
              // /curator-map, /broadcast, /subscribe. This array is ATH's, so a
              // number here is always a card.
              href: typeof n[1] === 'number' ? this.addr('ath-card', n[1])
                : n[1] === 'ath-all-releases' ? this.addr('releases')
                : n[1] === 'ath-artists' ? this.addr('artists')
                : null,
              // A card item lights when the deck is on that card; a page item
              // lights on its route, as before.
              style: typeof n[1] === 'number'
                ? menu(route === 'ath-map' && (st.panel || 0) === n[1] ? route : '\u0000')
                : menu(n[1]),
              click: typeof n[1] === 'number'
                ? this.navTo(() => this.goCard(n[1]))
                : this.navTo(() => this.goRoute(n[1]))
            }))
        // EVERY LABEL NAV ITEM IS A GRID PAGE. Releases opens the catalogue grid,
        // not the star field — the map is a mode you choose from the toggle, not
        // where the nav drops you. Artists is its own wall; the rest are flat.
        // ══ IN MAP MODE THE MENU FILLS THE COLUMN (2026-09-09, Tom) ═══════════
        // "Right now, if I'm in map mode and I hit the hamburger and hit any of
        // the buttons it goes back to grid mode. The site for a long time had no
        // grid mode — everything showed up in the sidebar of the star map."
        //
        // He is right that the views still exist, and that is what makes this
        // small: isArtists, isResults, isTour, isNews and isAlbum are all live
        // drawer blocks with their own data (artists as a LIST, not the photo
        // wall — his point exactly), and goArtists has carried the pattern all
        // along: `if (isStar) this.go('artists'); else this.goRoute('artists')`.
        // One item had the rule and the other five did not.
        //
        // So the branch generalises that single line rather than adding a
        // mechanism: on the star map every item calls go(view) and stays in the
        // map; off it, every item routes to its page as before. Releases keeps
        // its own exception in the off-map arm — the nav means the catalogue
        // grid there, per the 2026-09-04 note above.
        //
        // THE HREF DOES NOT CHANGE, deliberately. A middle-click or a copied
        // link should still give the visitor the standalone page — addr() is the
        // one place an address is written (rule 3) and a drawer view has no
        // address of its own. navTo() cancels the browser for a left click, so
        // the two behaviours coexist on one anchor exactly as they do elsewhere.
        //
        // ⚠ TWO VIEWS HAD NO COLUMN and are added with this: About had an
        // isAbout key with no markup behind it, and Radio had no drawer view at
        // all. Both would have been menu items that did nothing on the map.
        : LABEL_MENU
            .map(n => ({ key: n[1], label: n[0], style: menu(n[1]),
              href: this.addr(n[1]),
              click: this.navTo(() => isStar
                ? this.go(n[1])
                : this.goRoute(n[1] === 'releases' ? 'catalogue' : n[1])) })),
      subscribePillStyle: narrow ? { display: 'none' } : athSite
        ? {
            // ── IT MATCHES BROWSE, NOT ITS OWN NUMBER (2026-09-09, Tom) ──────
            // A fixed 32px while browseBtnStyle steps to 28 in the snug tier, so
            // at narrow widths Subscribe stood 4px taller than the run it sits
            // beside and the two crowded each other. Every other control in this
            // row already carries the tier; this one was written before it
            // existed and nothing tied it back.
            //
            // Height, padding and radius all read Browse's values rather than
            // restating them approximately — the pill's job is to be the same
            // box with a different border.
            display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center',
            height: snug ? '28px' : '32px', padding: snug ? '0 12px' : '0 14px',
            boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '5px', cursor: 'pointer', flexShrink: 0,
            border: '1px solid rgba(138,106,18,0.6)',
            background: route === 'ath-subscribe' ? 'rgba(138,106,18,0.14)' : 'transparent',
            color: '#8a6a12', fontWeight: route === 'ath-subscribe' ? 700 : 400, whiteSpace: 'nowrap'
          }
        : { display: 'none' },
      // ── TEN DEAD KEYS, DELETED (2026-09-09) ────────────────────────────────
      // mReleases / mArtists / mTour / mNews / mAbout and goReleases / goArtists
      // / goTour / goNews / goAbout: a retired nav block. Nothing in the
      // template read any of them — the live lists are navItems (desktop) and
      // menuItems (the hamburger).
      //
      // ⚠ AND THEY COST TOM A ROUND TRIP. Looking for the menu's handlers I
      // found goArtists' `if (isStar) this.go('artists')` and took it for the
      // live pattern to generalise. It was the right idea in a dead key, so I
      // wired the desktop nav, reported it done, and the hamburger — a third
      // list I had not found — still routed to grid pages. Ten plausible-looking
      // keys that nothing calls are worse than none, because they answer the
      // question "where is this handled" wrongly and with conviction.
      //
      // Deleted, not left defined: the file's own rule is that a key with no
      // markup reads as intent, and the intent here was abandonment.

      isStar: isStar,
      isLanding: route === 'landing',
      // Desktop bar: top 20 + 46 = 66. Phone bar: top 48 + 44 = 92. The pane
      // must start below whichever is in play, with a little air.
      flatPaneStyle: {
        position: 'absolute', top: narrow ? (PH_PANE + 'px') : '82px', left: 0, right: 0, bottom: 0,
        // THE TRANSPORT RESERVE IS CONDITIONAL (2026-09-02). This was a flat
        // 104px on every flat page, including the ones where no bar renders —
        // the same error DQ-22 named on the map's nav clearance, and the reason
        // the ATH cards had 574px of a 900px frame to fit into where the deck
        // gives them nearly all of it. No bar, no reserve.
        // barBand + 16 — see barBand. Was the literal '104px', correct only
        // because 88 + 16 happens to be 104 on a phone.
        paddingBottom: barShown ? ((barBand + 16) + 'px') : '24px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
        overflowY: 'auto', overflowX: 'hidden', scrollbarWidth: 'none'
      },
      // ── Clandestine: one shell, five pages ────────────────────────────────
      // Its own paper and its own typography — condensed Helvetica display at a
      // huge size over a warm grey — so it reads as a separate company from the
      // label sites, which is what it is. The palette is pinned locally on the
      // pane rather than through the site theme.
      isCls: clsPage != null,
      clsSubword: clsPage ? clsPage.subword : '',
      // clsPressFeed RETIRED — it gated the band to PR alone. Every Clandestine
      // page carries coverage now, so there is nothing to gate. The pressFeed
      // key still sits in CLS_PAGES and nothing reads it; left there rather than
      // deleted because the BUILD may key its Juicer wiring off it, and that is
      // a question for them, not a deletion for me. Build side: the answer is
      // every page, so either set them all true or drop the flag with this one.
      //
      // The band is a PLACEHOLDER for the live Juicer feed, and says so per
      // page rather than in one generic sentence — a reader on the Radio page
      // should see what will appear on the Radio page.
      //
      // ⚠ EACH PAGE STATES ITS OWN PHRASE. This was derived by lowercasing the
      // page's subword, which printed "pr placements" on the PR page — the
      // flagship page for this band — because PR is an initialism, and "label
      // services placements" on the parent. A phrase built by transforming a
      // label will always break on the label that does not survive transforming.
      // Each page now says what actually lands on it, and management, distro,
      // fulfillment and manufacturing say "press for …" or "reviews and
      // features for …" rather than claiming placements they do not make.
      //
      // ⚠ EACH PHRASE IS A COMPLETE CLAUSE, and the stem no longer says
      // "coverage". Two faults, and they compounded: a shared "… as they land"
      // tail turned the four mass-noun phrasings ungrammatical ("coverage for
      // the records we make as they land"), and "coverage" landed three times
      // in twenty words — the band head, the stem, and the noun. The tail now
      // belongs to whichever page can carry it.
      clsPaneStyle: {
        // TOP 0, not the flat pane's 82/70: that offset exists to clear the
        // label header bar, and Clandestine renders none — so it was reserving a
        // band for an element that no longer exists, showing the stage's white
        // through above the warm-grey paper. The sealed site owns the whole
        // frame, and its own top bar is sticky at 0.
        position: 'absolute', top: 0, left: 0, right: 0, bottom: 0,
        paddingBottom: '80px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
        overflowY: 'auto', overflowX: 'hidden', scrollbarWidth: 'none',
        background: clsPaper, color: clsInk,
        fontFamily: '"Helvetica Neue", Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif',
        fontSize: '16px', lineHeight: 1.55
      },
      clsWrapStyle: { padding: '0 ' + (narrow ? 20 : Math.round(Math.min(80, Math.max(32, W * 0.05)))) + 'px', boxSizing: 'border-box', maxWidth: '100%', overflowX: 'hidden' },
      // The five pages as one row of tabs. Upstream this is the Explore
      // switcher in the top nav; here it is inline, because the design document
      // needs all five reachable without a second chrome layer.
      // ── One line, not two ────────────────────────────────────────────────
      // Eight items in the bar wrapped it to two rows on desktop and three on a
      // phone. They live in a dropdown now — the bar stays one line at every
      // width, and the panel can say what a row of chips could not: which page
      // you are on, which pages exist, and which of these leave the site.
      clsMenuOpen: !!st.clsMenu,
      // HOVER, like every other menu in the file — open on enter, close on leave.
      // Click-to-open/click-again-to-close made a bar menu behave like a modal.
      // Opening one closes the other, so the two panels can never overlap.
      // ── TAP, NOT ONLY HOVER (2026-09-05, item 2) ──────────────────────────
      // These were hover-only, and a touch device has no hover — so on a
      // tablet the two Clandestine menus could not be opened at all. Same
      // mistake as the curator-map dots (r17) and the same fix: hover and tap
      // are different gestures, so the trigger carries a click as well, and
      // the click TOGGLES (a tap must be able to close what it opened, which
      // is the one case where a toggle is right).
      // ── ⚠ FOURTH ATTEMPT, AND THE FIRST THAT DOES NOT ASSUME GEOMETRY
      // (2026-09-06) ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      // Measured live: the button is 116×25 at y 12–37, the panel renders at
      // y 270, and the hover region renders at EXACTLY the button's box. My
      // three previous fixes all tried to make the region enclose the panel —
      // but the panel is absolutely positioned, so it contributes nothing to
      // its parent's height and the region can never grow to cover it. Every
      // one of those attempts was reasoning about a box instead of measuring
      // it, which is the same mistake the card centring took three reports to
      // shake.
      //
      // So stop trying to bridge the gap with a box. Two changes that hold
      // wherever the panel lands:
      //   1. THE PANEL HOLDS ITSELF OPEN. It had no onMouseEnter at all, while
      //      the Explore panel beside it always had one — that asymmetry is
      //      the whole bug. Hovering the panel now keeps it open regardless of
      //      how far it is from the button.
      //   2. CLOSING IS DELAYED, through the same panelLeave/panelHold pair
      //      the label header's Explore has used since it was built. 220ms is
      //      long enough to cross any gap and short enough not to feel sticky.
      // Neither depends on knowing where the panel renders, which is why this
      // one will survive the next layout change.
      clsMenuOn: () => { this.panelHold(); this.setState({ clsMenu: true, explore: false }); },
      clsMenuOff: () => this.panelLeave('clsMenu'),
      clsMenuHold: () => this.panelHold(),
      clsMenuTap: (e) => { if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation(); this.setState({ clsMenu: !st.clsMenu, explore: false }); },
      clsExpTap: (e) => { if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation(); this.setState({ explore: !st.explore, clsMenu: false }); },
      clsExpBtnStyle: {
        display: 'inline-flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '0.5em', cursor: 'pointer',
        padding: '2px 0', whiteSpace: 'nowrap',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '10.5px',
        letterSpacing: '0.22em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
        color: st.explore ? clsGold : clsMuted
      },
      clsExpChevStyle: { fontSize: '8px', transform: st.explore ? 'rotate(180deg)' : 'none', transition: 'transform 160ms ease' },
      // The two menus sit together at the right end of the bar.
      clsBarRightStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: narrow ? '18px' : '28px', flexShrink: 0 },
      // The four header controls are a desktop row — at 390 they ran 400px past
      // the stage edge. On narrow they go into the drawer instead (menuItems),
      // which is what the hamburger beside them opens (2026-09-05).
      // The region that keeps the services menu open — the button and its panel
      // together (2026-09-06).
      //
      // ⚠ NEVER display:none HERE. The panel is INSIDE this region, and at
      // narrow that panel is a position:fixed bottom sheet — but display:none
      // on an ancestor removes a fixed child from rendering all the same. That
      // is the exact r34 regression the markup's own comment warns about, and
      // hiding the region reintroduced it: the sheet rendered with a zero rect.
      // So the region always renders and the BUTTON is what hides at narrow
      // (clsMenuWrapStyle below), which is what was hidden on phones before.
      // position: static at narrow so the region cannot become a containing
      // block for the fixed sheet, and no width of its own so it contributes
      // nothing to the phone header's flex row.
      clsMenuRegionStyle: narrow
        ? { position: 'static', width: 0, flexShrink: 0 }
        : { position: 'relative', flexShrink: 0 },
      clsMenuWrapStyle: narrow ? { display: 'none' } : { position: 'relative', flexShrink: 0 },
      clsMenuBtnStyle: {
        display: 'inline-flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '0.5em', cursor: 'pointer',
        padding: '2px 0', whiteSpace: 'nowrap',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '10.5px',
        letterSpacing: '0.22em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
        color: st.clsMenu ? clsGold : clsMuted
      },
      clsChevStyle: { fontSize: '8px', transform: st.clsMenu ? 'rotate(180deg)' : 'none', transition: 'transform 160ms ease' },
      // ── THE CLANDESTINE EXPLORE PANEL, ON THE SERVICES ANCHOR (2026-09-06) ─
      // ⚠ THIRD ATTEMPT AT ONE COMPLAINT, so this one removes the possibility
      // rather than the symptom. The two menus kept opening at different
      // heights even after both were given top: 100% and the same 10px drop,
      // because they were still two different style objects on two different
      // wrappers — and any of a dozen things (a padding, a border, an inline
      // box's line-height) can make two "identical" offsets resolve apart.
      //
      // Explore on Clandestine now spreads CLS_PANEL_ANCHOR, the very object
      // Our services uses. Not a matching value — the same one. They cannot
      // differ, and there is no third place to keep in step.
      //
      // The label header keeps explorePanelStyle: it is a different bar with
      // one menu on it, and nothing there to be consistent with.
      clsExplorePanelStyle: Object.assign({}, CLS_PANEL_ANCHOR, {
        width: '372px',
        padding: '18px 0 8px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
        background: clsPaper, border: '1px solid ' + clsRule,
        boxShadow: '0 26px 70px rgba(0,0,0,0.28)',
        display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column'
      }),
      clsMenuPanelStyle: narrow
        // ── A SHEET ON A PHONE (2026-09-05, Tom) ─────────────────────────────
        // The seven services had been flattened into the ☰ drawer as seven
        // flat rows, which is what muddied it: a set of destinations that is a
        // DROPDOWN on desktop should not become a list on a phone. One panel
        // for both now — anchored under the button at width, a bottom sheet at
        // narrow, which is the shape Browse and Explore already use there, so
        // all three of Clandestine's menus behave alike.
        ? {
            position: 'fixed', left: 0, right: 0, bottom: 0, zIndex: 80,
            maxHeight: '72vh', overflowY: 'auto',
            padding: '12px 0 22px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
            background: clsPaper, borderTop: '1px solid ' + clsRule,
            boxShadow: '0 -26px 70px rgba(0,0,0,0.28)',
            display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column'
          }
        : Object.assign({}, CLS_PANEL_ANCHOR, {
        width: '272px',
        padding: '12px 0 6px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
        background: clsPaper, border: '1px solid ' + clsRule,
        boxShadow: '0 26px 70px rgba(0,0,0,0.28)',
        display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column'
      }),
      clsMenuSectionStyle: {
        padding: '8px 16px 5px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px',
        letterSpacing: '0.28em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: clsMuted, opacity: 0.75
      },
      // clsMenuHere DELETED 2026-09-03 with the "This page" section; the current
      // page is marked in clsMenuPages instead.
      // ALL SEVEN SERVICES ARE PAGES HERE NOW. Manufacturing, Distro and
      // Fulfillment used to sit under an "Other sites" heading pointing at their
      // own domains — the in-page menu routed straight past the site. The
      // outbound domain belongs on the destination page instead.
      clsMenuPages: ['cls-pr', 'cls-radio', 'cls-distribution', 'cls-fulfillment', 'cls-manufacturing', 'cls-management', 'cls-licensing']
        // The seven SERVICES. 'cls' (the parent page) is not in the list — it is the
        // masthead you click to get home, not a service. The page you are on stays
        // in the list with an orange dot; it is not hoisted out or repeated.
        .map(h => {
          const here = h === route;
          return {
            key: h,
            name: this.CLS_PAGES[h].subword,

      // THE OFFSITE LINK LIVES ON ITS PAGE, not in the menu. It sits in the empty

      clsCtaWrapStyle: (clsPage && clsPage.cta && clsPage.cta.length)
        ? { gridColumn: narrow ? '1' : '3', gridRow: narrow ? 'auto' : '1', display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: '8px', alignSelf: 'start' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      // A SQUARE TILE, not a text link — it is the one outbound action on the
      // page and it sits alone in the margin, so it can afford the weight.
      clsCtas: ((clsPage && clsPage.cta) || []).map((c, i) => ({
        key: 'cta' + i, label: c[0], href: c[1],
        // NOT A BUTTON. These pages are paper, hairline rules and mono labels

        // a UI chip imported from somewhere else. This is a masthead item
        // instead: a gold rule, the action set in type under it, the domain
        // beneath that. It uses the page's own vocabulary, and the gold rule is

        // No printed URL — an arrow says it opens a site, which is the standing
        // rule everywhere else on these pages. It was also the longest element
        // here, and in a fixed-width block a single unbreakable domain token
        // cannot shrink, so it overhung the rule and pushed the whole focus band
        // 14px wider than its container.
        box: {
          display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', alignItems: 'flex-start',
          width: narrow ? '100%' : 'max-content', maxWidth: narrow ? '100%' : '196px',
          boxSizing: 'border-box',
          paddingTop: '13px', borderTop: '2px solid ' + clsGold,
          textDecoration: 'none', color: 'inherit'
        },
        labelStyle: {
          fontFamily: 'inherit', fontSize: '17px', fontWeight: 600,
          letterSpacing: '-0.015em', lineHeight: 1.3, color: clsInk, textWrap: 'pretty'

      clsMastStyle: {
        // The mark sits close under the bar. The old top pad scaled to 112px on

        padding: (narrow ? 18 : Math.round(Math.min(48, W * 0.032))) + 'px 0 ' + (narrow ? 26 : Math.round(Math.min(56, W * 0.05))) + 'px',
        display: 'grid', gap: (narrow ? 22 : 34) + 'px', minWidth: 0
      },
      // Painted through a mask, so the file's own ink is irrelevant and the mark

      //
      // THE IDEA: the Google form asks all 34 service questions of everyone.

        const edge = hx(ac, on ? 0.95 : 0.34);

          },
          // KEYBOARD-REACHABLE (2026-09-02). These were div/span with onClick
          // only, so a keyboard user could type their name and email and could
          // not tick a single service — the entire point of the form. Native
          // <input type=checkbox> would bring a UA-drawn control this page has
          // no vocabulary for, so the row IS the checkbox: role, aria-checked,
          // tabindex, and Space/Enter running the same handler as the pointer.
          on: on, ariaChecked: on ? 'true' : 'false',
          click: () => {
            const nx = Object.assign({}, st.clsSvc);
            if (nx[si]) delete nx[si]; else nx[si] = 1;
            this.setState({ clsSvc: nx });
          },
          keyDown: (e) => {
            if (e.key !== ' ' && e.key !== 'Enter') return;
            e.preventDefault();
            const nx = Object.assign({}, st.clsSvc);
            if (nx[si]) delete nx[si]; else nx[si] = 1;
            this.setState({ clsSvc: nx });
          },
          // A SQUARE THAT FILLS, not a tick in a rounded box. The page has no
          // rounded corners anywhere and one control should not introduce them.
          mark: {
            width: '13px', height: '13px', flexShrink: 0, boxSizing: 'border-box',
            border: '1px solid ' + (on ? tickCol : edge),
            background: on ? tickCol : 'transparent'
          },
          nameStyle: {
            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '11px',
            letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: clsInk
          },
          descStyle: {
            marginTop: '7px', fontSize: '13px', lineHeight: 1.5, color: clsReadInk,
            display: on ? 'none' : 'block'
          },
          // The count replaces the descriptor once the tile is open: at that
          // point what you need to know is how many you have said yes to.
          countStyle: {
            marginTop: '7px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '10px',
            letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
            color: picked ? tickCol : clsMuted, display: on ? 'block' : 'none'
          },
          count: picked ? (picked + (picked === 1 ? ' selected' : ' selected')) : 'Select what applies',
          qsWrapStyle: on
            ? { display: 'grid', gap: '2px', marginTop: '14px', paddingTop: '13px', borderTop: '1px solid ' + clsRule }
            : { display: 'none' },
          qs: s[2].map((q, qi) => {
            const qon = !!st.clsPick[si + ':' + qi];
            return {
              key: 'q' + si + '_' + qi, label: q,
              box: {
                display: 'flex', alignItems: 'flex-start', gap: '10px', padding: '7px 0',
                cursor: 'pointer', minWidth: 0
              },
              mark: {
                width: '11px', height: '11px', flexShrink: 0, marginTop: '3px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
                border: '1px solid ' + (qon ? tickCol : clsRule),
                background: qon ? tickCol : 'transparent'
              },
              labelStyle: {
                fontSize: '12.5px', lineHeight: 1.45, minWidth: 0,
                // Readable BEFORE the tick, not after it. The square is the
                // state signal; the sentence is what you read to decide.
                color: qon ? clsInk : clsReadInk, textWrap: 'pretty'
              },
              ariaChecked: qon ? 'true' : 'false',
              click: (e) => {
                if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
                const nx = Object.assign({}, st.clsPick), k = si + ':' + qi;
                if (nx[k]) delete nx[k]; else nx[k] = 1;
                this.setState({ clsPick: nx });
              },
              keyDown: (e) => {
                if (e.key !== ' ' && e.key !== 'Enter') return;
                e.preventDefault();
                if (e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
                const nx = Object.assign({}, st.clsPick), k = si + ':' + qi;
                if (nx[k]) delete nx[k]; else nx[k] = 1;
                this.setState({ clsPick: nx });
              }
            };
          })
        };
      }),
      // THE CONTACT BLOCK ARRIVES WITH THE FIRST TICK. Asking a stranger for
      // their name before they have said what they want is the thing the long
      // form does; here the questions earn the details.
      // BELOW THE GRID, AND ONLY ONCE A BOX IS TICKED (2026-09-02, Tom).
      // Three passes. It began below and gated; moved above and permanent when
      // it was too heavy to ignore and too low to notice; and is back below and
      // gated now that it is light enough to be a footnote rather than a form.
      // The pass above the grid was not wasted — it is why this is a four-track
      // strip at 12.5px instead of the two-column block it started as.
      //
      // The ask is the grid. The details are what you give once you have made
      // one, so they arrive as a reply to the tick rather than a gate before it.
      clsFormFieldsStyle: Object.keys(st.clsSvc).length
        ? { display: 'grid',
            gridTemplateColumns: narrow ? '1fr' : (W < 1000 ? 'repeat(2, minmax(0, 1fr))' : 'repeat(4, minmax(0, 1fr))'),
            gap: narrow ? '14px' : '14px 22px',
            paddingTop: narrow ? '24px' : '30px',
            borderTop: '1px solid ' + clsRule }
        : { display: 'none' },
      clsAreaGrow: (e) => { const el = e.target; el.style.height = '30px'; el.style.height = Math.max(30, el.scrollHeight) + 'px'; },
      // ── SEND EMITS, AND THE FIELDS ARE IN STATE (2026-09-04, designer
      // request 14) ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      // Send was a drawn span with no action, so the host attached a temporary
      // listener to make the form work at all. It goes through the standard
      // action channel now — emit('form.submit', …) → window.__DC_ON_ACTION —
      // and carries what the visitor entered: every field value by its key,
      // plus the ticked services and their ticked questions. The host's
      // temporary bridge can be deleted.
      clsFormSend: () => {
        // A sent form must not re-fire; a failed one must be retryable.
        if (st.clsSending || st.clsSent) return;
        const svcs = Object.keys(st.clsSvc || {}).filter(k => st.clsSvc[k]).map(k => (this.CLS_FORM[k] || [])[0] || k);
        const asks = Object.keys(st.clsPick || {}).filter(k => st.clsPick[k]).map(k => {
          const p = String(k).split(':');
          const svc = this.CLS_FORM[p[0]] || [];
          return (svc[0] || p[0]) + ' \u00b7 ' + ((svc[2] || [])[p[1]] || p[1]);
        });
        this.emit('form.submit', {
          source: 'clandestine', form: 'enquiry',
          fields: Object.assign({}, st.clsFields || {}),
          services: svcs, questions: asks
        });
        // Not "sent" — SENDING. The host answers through __DC_FORM_ACK, wired
        // in componentDidMount; until it does, the control says Sending….
        this.setState({ clsSending: true, clsSent: false, clsFail: false });
        // If the host never acknowledges, the form must not sit on "Sending…"
        // for ever. After 6s it closes and resets as though delivered, because
        // by then the enquiry has either arrived or been lost on their side —
        // and a form stuck mid-send invites the double submission the reset
        // exists to prevent (2026-09-06).
        setTimeout(() => {
          if (!this.state.clsSending) return;
          this.setState({ clsSending: false, clsSent: true, clsFormOpen: false, clsFields: {}, clsSvc: {}, clsPick: {} });
        }, 6000);
      },
      clsFormFields: this.CLS_FORM_FIELDS.map((f) => ({
        // The emit carries values, so the design has to hold them: one key per
        // field, written on input. The textarea keeps its auto-grow as well.
        onInput: (e) => {
          const v = e && e.target ? e.target.value : '';
          this.setState({ clsFields: Object.assign({}, this.state.clsFields || {}, { [f[0]]: v }) });
        },
        onArea: (e) => {
          const v = e && e.target ? e.target.value : '';
          const el = e && e.target;
          if (el) { el.style.height = 'auto'; el.style.height = Math.max(30, el.scrollHeight) + 'px'; }
          this.setState({ clsFields: Object.assign({}, this.state.clsFields || {}, { [f[0]]: v }) });
        },
        key: 'cff' + f[0], label: f[1] + (f[2] ? ' *' : ''), note: f[4], type: f[3],
        // Row 1 is Name · Email · Genres · Release date, all single tracks.
        // Row 2 is the description across two, then the link and the referral.
        wrap: { display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: '4px', minWidth: 0,
                gridColumn: f[3] === 'area' && !narrow ? 'span 2' : 'auto' },
        labelStyle: {
          fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8px',
          letterSpacing: '0.18em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: clsMuted,
          whiteSpace: 'nowrap', overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis'
        },
        // A RULED LINE, NOT A BOX. Boxed inputs would be the only enclosed
        // shapes on a page built entirely from hairlines and bands.
        inputType: f[3] === 'date' ? 'date' : f[3] === 'email' ? 'email' : 'text',
        ph: f[3] === 'date' ? '' : '',
        input: f[3] === 'area' ? { display: 'none' } : {
          width: '100%', boxSizing: 'border-box', padding: '6px 0',
          border: 'none', borderBottom: '1px solid ' + clsRule, background: 'transparent',
          fontFamily: 'inherit', fontSize: '12.5px', lineHeight: 1.45, color: clsInk,
          outline: 'none'
        },
        // THE SAME HEIGHT AS ITS ROW-MATES. It shares row one with Name and
        // Email, and a 44px box beside two 30px rules made the row look broken
        // rather than deliberate. It is now one ruled line like the others and
        // grows as you type — the strip stays two even rows either way.
        area: f[3] !== 'area' ? { display: 'none' } : {
          width: '100%', boxSizing: 'border-box', padding: '6px 0', height: '30px', minHeight: '30px',
          border: 'none', borderBottom: '1px solid ' + clsRule, background: 'transparent',
          fontFamily: 'inherit', fontSize: '12.5px', lineHeight: 1.45, color: clsInk,
          outline: 'none', resize: 'vertical', overflow: 'hidden'
        },
        noteStyle: f[4]
          ? { fontSize: '10.5px', lineHeight: 1.45, color: clsMuted }
          : { display: 'none' }
      })),
      // Gated with the fields it submits — a send button above an empty form
      // is a button for nothing.
      // ── FOUR STATES, FOUR LOOKS (2026-09-04, r5 ask 1) ────────────────────
      // The label changed but the control did not, so at a glance a failed send
      // still read as a finished one. Ready = solid ink. Sending = muted, not
      // clickable. Sent = quiet and outlined, done. Failed = outlined in the
      // warning tone and clickable again, because the visitor's next move is to
      // retry. Which one is the host's to say, through __DC_FORM_ACK.
      clsFormSendStyle: Object.keys(st.clsSvc).length ? (() => {
        const base = {
          justifySelf: 'start', display: 'inline-block', padding: '0.85rem 1.9rem',
          boxSizing: 'border-box',
          fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '11px',
          letterSpacing: '0.2em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
          transition: 'background 200ms linear, color 200ms linear'
        };
        if (st.clsFail) return Object.assign(base, { background: 'transparent', color: '#a8321e', border: '1px solid #a8321e', cursor: 'pointer' });
        if (st.clsSent) return Object.assign(base, { background: 'transparent', color: clsMuted, border: '1px solid ' + clsRule, cursor: 'default' });
        if (st.clsSending) return Object.assign(base, { background: clsRule, color: clsPaper, cursor: 'progress' });
        return Object.assign(base, { background: clsInk, color: clsPaper, cursor: 'pointer' });
      })() : { display: 'none' },
      clsCloseBandStyle: { borderTop: '1px solid ' + clsRule, padding: (narrow ? 30 : Math.round(Math.min(72, Math.max(44, W * 0.06)))) + 'px 0 0', minWidth: 0 },
      // .cls-close is a grid with a 1.75rem gap, and the line is UPPERCASE
      // condensed 800 at 18ch — mine was sentence case, weight 700, 30ch.
      clsCloseInnerStyle: { display: 'grid', gap: '1.75rem' },
      clsCloseLineStyle: {
        fontFamily: '"Helvetica Neue", Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif', fontWeight: 800, fontStretch: 'condensed',
        fontSize: Math.round(Math.max(28, Math.min(68, W * 0.058))) + 'px', lineHeight: 0.95,
        letterSpacing: '-0.025em', textTransform: 'uppercase', maxWidth: '18ch', margin: 0, textWrap: 'balance'
      },
      clsCloseBodyStyle: { margin: 0, fontSize: '1.05rem', lineHeight: 1.6, maxWidth: '64ch' },
      clsCtaStyle: {
        justifySelf: 'start', display: 'inline-block', padding: '1.05rem 2.25rem',
        boxSizing: 'border-box', background: clsInk, color: clsPaper, cursor: 'pointer',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '12px',
        letterSpacing: '0.22em', textTransform: 'uppercase'
      },
      clsCloseMailStyle: {
        margin: '-0.75rem 0 0', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '11px',
        letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: clsMuted,
        borderBottom: '1px solid ' + clsRule, paddingBottom: '2px', display: 'inline-block'
      },
      // Upstream footer: LOCATIONS.join(' · ') plus the contact line.
      clsFooterStyle: {
        borderTop: '1px solid ' + clsRule, marginTop: narrow ? '30px' : '44px',
        padding: '1.5rem 0 1rem', display: 'flex', flexWrap: 'wrap', gap: '8px 24px',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '10px',
        letterSpacing: narrow ? '0.16em' : '0.24em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: clsMuted
      },
      // "See our work ↓" — the same type as the two menu triggers beside it so
      // the row reads as one set; the gold down-arrow is the only tell.
      clsWorkBtnStyle: {
        display: narrow ? 'none' : 'inline-flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '0.5em', cursor: 'pointer',
        padding: '2px 0', whiteSpace: 'nowrap',

        letterSpacing: '0.22em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: clsMuted
      },
      clsWorkArrowStyle: { fontSize: '10px', color: clsGold, lineHeight: 1 },

      clsPortalBtnStyle: {
        display: narrow ? 'none' : 'inline-flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '0.5em', cursor: 'pointer',
        padding: '2px 0', whiteSpace: 'nowrap', textDecoration: 'none',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '10.5px',
        letterSpacing: '0.22em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: clsMuted

      clsPortalArrowStyle: { fontSize: '10px', color: clsGold, lineHeight: 1 },
      clsSeeWork: () => this.clsSeeWorkNow(),

      // Square tiles, four across on desktop, two on a phone. The grid is the

      clsWorkGridStyle: { display: 'grid', gap: narrow ? '6px' : '10px', gridTemplateColumns: 'repeat(' + (narrow ? 2 : 4) + ', minmax(0, 1fr))' },

      clsTopbarStyle: {
        position: 'sticky', top: 0, zIndex: 70, background: clsPaper,

        padding: '12px ' + (narrow ? 20 : Math.round(Math.min(80, Math.max(32, W * 0.05)))) + 'px',
        borderBottom: '1px solid ' + clsRule, boxSizing: 'border-box'
      },
      // ── THE SLOGAN FITS ON A PHONE (2026-09-05, Tom) ──────────────────────
      // It was `nowrap` with an ellipsis at narrow, so a 49-character sentence
      // in tracked mono was cut to "…HELPING LABELS AND A…" — a slogan that
      // stops mid-word says less than no slogan, and it is the first line of
      // type on the page.
      //
      // Two lines rather than smaller type: at 390 the bar has the height for
      // it (the hamburger is 44 tall and sets the row), and dropping the size
      // far enough to fit 49 characters on one line would have put it under
      // 8px. So wrap, tighten the tracking to 0.06em (tracking is what makes
      // mono uppercase expensive — 0.1em on 49 characters is roughly five
      // characters' worth of width), and let it FLEX into the row's own space
      // rather than capping it in ch: a 25ch cap held it to 127px and pushed it
      // to three lines with 170px of the bar empty beside it. flex:1 with
      // min-width:0 takes exactly what the hamburger leaves.
      clsTaglineStyle: narrow
        ? {
            margin: 0, flex: 1, minWidth: 0,
            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8.5px',
            lineHeight: 1.5, letterSpacing: '0.06em',
            textTransform: 'uppercase', color: clsInk, textWrap: 'pretty'
          }
        : {
            margin: 0, minWidth: 0, width: '100%',
            overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis', whiteSpace: 'normal',
            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '10.5px',
            letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: clsInk
          },
      // clsReadInk, not clsMuted — same rule as the form's question text, and
      // the same defect: 3.0:1 italic on tinted paper. It was tolerable while

      // ── §4-4 / §4-5 · THE STOP FRAMES ────────────────────────────────
      // One frame, two routes. Everything that varies by label — the mark, the
      // nav, the paper, the ink — is the header's and the theme's already, so
      // the frame itself is label-agnostic and there is nothing per-label to
      // supply. That is the whole reason to draw it here rather than five times.
      // Reads STOP_ROUTES rather than restating it (2026-09-09): the list gates
      // stopFrameRoute() and the SITE_REQUIRED guard, and a third stop route added
      // there would have suppressed the guard while drawing nothing.
      isStop: this.STOP_ROUTES.indexOf(route) > -1,
      stopSurface: route === 'error' ? 'error' : 'notfound',
      stopPaneStyle: {
        position: 'absol
